## Phase 1B B2 — Additive read-only effectiveRights response

The version 1 `effectiveRights` contract is an informational projection of the already tenant-scoped Python configured plan. Top-level `hasAccess` and subscription `status` remain authoritative for active subscription access. Items contain `featureCode`, `state`, raw Python `unit`, `aggregationType`, `resetCycle`, and `limit`; `state` disambiguates `absent`, `disabled`, `explicit_zero`, `limited`, and `unlimited`. Only `established_python_code` mappings are serialized. Candidate aliases, unmapped codes, Python-only controls, internal usage, and tenant identifiers are omitted. B2 reuses the plan already loaded by `get_subscription()` and makes no second plan read or accounting call.

## Phase 1B ENT-007 — Storage lifecycle review (2026-10-21)

| Phase | Capability | Path | Action | Change and preservation | Verification/status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1B-ENT-007 | ENT-007 | `backend/src/ai/core/storage_ledger.py`; `backend/src/ai/marketing/image_storage.py`; `backend/src/ai/extraction/minio_persistence.py`; `backend/src/ai/routers/marketing.py`; `backend/src/ai/extraction/ingestion_pipeline.py`; `backend/src/ai/routers/extraction.py`; focused storage tests | MODIFY / ADD | Adds confirmed-delete-before-ledger-release and failed-upload compensation helpers. Marketing upload failures now remove unreferenced objects or retain their bytes in the tenant ledger; campaign deletion returns the existing 502 error shape and preserves rows when object deletion fails; replacement cleanup releases old bytes only after deletion succeeds. RAG PDF registration writes its deterministic byte ledger entry before upload, then transfers accounting to `rag_documents_metadata`; caught failures remove the object or retain generic storage accounting. Image and extraction MinIO helpers now accept tenant-scoped pre-generated keys and an optional pre-upload ledger callback, support retries with the same key, and attempt cleanup after ambiguous PUT errors. Marketing image routes and the production extraction upload path now pass the callback; it commits through a separate tenant-scoped app-role connection before PUT. Stale recovery is lazy with a 24-hour grace: marketing rows are deleted only when unreferenced and object removal is confirmed; extraction rows are forgotten only when the specific object is confirmed absent. Missing buckets and storage errors retain accounting. No migration, quota/unit, entitlement, credit, or successful-response contract change. | py -3.12 -m unittest src.ai.tests.test_storage_upload_recovery src.ai.tests.test_storage_ledger in the temporary isolated Python environment: 15 passed; direct process_records callback-forwarding regression passed. Native PostgreSQL/RLS, MinIO, and crash-recovery verification remains NOT RUN because the disposable database and MinIO infrastructure were unavailable. AST syntax checks passed for changed Python files. ENT-007 remains PARTIAL pending native verification. |

### Decision reconciliation

- **U5 (ENT-001/002):** Established Python-code alignments are `market_intelligence`, `demand_prediction`, `competitor_management`, `sentiment_analysis`, `rag_assistant`, `document_extraction`, `marketing_image_generation`, `tracked_competitors`, `tracked_products`, `connected_data_sources`, and `document_storage_mb`. Crosswalk-only candidates are `market_perception` -> `market_intelligence`, `report_generation` -> `monthly_reports`, and `team_members` -> `team_seats`; none is activated as an independent grant. `dashboard_analytics`, `inventory_intelligence`, `product_management`, `data_integration`, `business_recommendations`, `system_alerts`, and `ai_pricing` have no distinct Python grant in the crosswalk and remain unmapped. Python-only `data_credits`, `ai_credits`, and `web_searches` remain separate. U5 blocks missing/ambiguous legacy backfill, new independent gates, and rollout until its cohort/contract/effective-date decisions; it does not block read-only reporting or established mappings.
- **U6 (ENT-006):** Current Python capacity counts active `OWN_SYSTEM_CONNECTOR`; one-time `CSV_UPLOAD` is excluded. Legacy website/GA4/competitor-monitoring records, duplicate identities, reconnect and disabled cases are not mapped by label alone. U6 blocks any cutover/migration that changes customer access, not preservation of current source rights.
- **U1-U4 (ENT-010/CREDIT):** U1 blocks new/changed feature-metering schedules (units, measured quantity, rounding, partial success, retained output and retry/cache behavior); U2-U3 block new uncertain-delivery allowance adjustment authority, ceilings, evidence, period and expiry; U4 blocks new credit conversions/terms. Existing Python schedules, immutable ledgers, provider spend and separate pools remain unchanged.
- **U7:** Blocks only new exceptional overlapping manual/Stripe grants and their precedence/expiry/reconciliation; normal Python-confirmed Stripe contract behavior remains reusable.
- U8's slice-level authorization is supplied by the user's prior B1-B4/ENT task approvals; it is not reopened here.

### Verification performed in this batch

- Disposable PostgreSQL 17.11 (`keel_billing_test`) preflight found zero public tables before migrations; fresh Final_schema plus migrations 1-91 applied. `ceopro_app` verified `rolsuper=false`, `rolbypassrls=false`. Restricted-role behavior was exercised through the native integration paths.
- Isolated MinIO real PUT/GET/DELETE smoke passed. Native marketing and extraction-result integration run: 11 passed; storage ledger/upload helper run: 15 passed; extraction/RAG database integration after test-harness correction: 4 passed.
- Focused entitlement/credit/billing invocation: 80 passed, 3 usage tests failed because the test run set `UNASSIGNED_PLAN_ACCESS=none`, contrary to the repository's fixture contract. Rerunning the affected usage file with the documented `default` setting passed 7/7. Those three initial failures were test-configuration-only; no production defect was demonstrated.
- The extraction/RAG file's initial run exposed stale test fixture expectations (missing alias, outdated MinIO mock call signature, and assertions that confused extraction JSON with RAG PDF objects); the focused test-only correction was verified by the subsequent 4/4 native-DB run. No production code changed.
- Disposable PostgreSQL/Redis/MinIO containers were removed after verification. Existing Phase 1A PostgreSQL/Redis and SearxNG containers were left running and verified. No external provider, production/shared DB, browser journey, or production migration was used. Prior ENT-008 (158 + 24), ENT-009 (8), B3 and B4 evidence was not rerun without a regression trigger.

### Phase 1B disposition and Phase 2A gate

Phase 1B remains **PARTIALLY COMPLETE**, not unconditionally closed: U5/U6 still block access-affecting mappings/backfills, ENT-007 recovery is lazy rather than unattended and process-kill recovery was not directly simulated, ENT-010's new metering/remedy cases are U1-U3-gated, and ENT-011 browser compatibility remains unverified. No production defect was demonstrated by this batch; the initial failures were isolated to test configuration and stale test fixtures, then the focused affected tests passed after correction.

A bounded Phase 2A development slice can begin only if it inventories or manages catalog/history using the current Python catalog and preserves all existing prices, trial terms, Stripe mappings, private contracts, subscriptions and rights. Do not publish changed commercial values, change a customer's effective rights, backfill missing grants, alter source counting, or implement exceptional manual/Stripe overlaps until the applicable U5/U6/U7 decision and impact review are complete. A broader 2A transition/activation slice needs its own implementation contract and explicit approval. No Phase 2 work is authorized by this closeout.

## Phase 1B ENT-009 implementation update (2026-10-21)

This update supersedes only the ENT-009 gap/status above; the earlier closeout and its test outcomes remain historical evidence. Authority: `../KEEL_PHASE1B_DECISION_HANDOFF/PHASE1B_APPROVED_REQUIREMENTS.md` (ENT-009), `PHASE1B_ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md` (AT-ENT-009), `originals/python-docs/architecture/ADR-ENT-001-feature-rights-units-and-credit-crosswalk.md`, and `originals/python-docs/architecture/FEATURE-entitlements.md`. The approved technical behavior was shared invalidation plus exact existing trial expiry; no new grace period, plan, entitlement, or customer term was introduced.

| Requirement | Files | Change and preservation | Verification/status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ENT-009 / AT-ENT-009 | `backend/src/ai/core/entitlements.py`; `backend/src/infrastructure/database/migrations/20261028000100_entitlement_version_tracking.sql`; `backend/tests/entitlements/test_cache.py` | Adds tenant and shared catalog generation counters advanced transactionally by assignment, trial, override, plan, limit, feature, and included-entitlement DML. Existing tenant rows are seeded at generation 1. `entitlements._plan()` verifies both counters through the existing restricted tenant connection before using process-local data; a failed check raises and cannot serve the cached plan. Trial cache entries are bypassed at the existing `billing_trials.ends_at` timestamp. `usage.get_plan`, `requires`, status/feature meaning, Redis authentication behavior, grandfathered rights, billing, credits, and API contracts are unchanged. | Fresh disposable PostgreSQL 17.11 on `127.0.0.1:55440`; empty target identity check PASS; migrations 1–88 and new migration applied (89 migration files total); restricted `ceopro_app` preflight PASS (`rolsuper=false`, `rolbypassrls=false`); focused `tests/entitlements/test_cache.py` — **8 passed**. Covered cancellation, plan change across independent cache instances, exact trial expiry without mutation, version-store failure, tenant RLS, four concurrent assignment updates, shared catalog generation, and override generation. Disposable Compose project and temporary environment removed. |

Performance/security notes: every authorization cache hit adds one indexed version query on the already pooled, tenant-scoped connection; full plan reads remain cached. Version reads do not lock. Mutation triggers serialize increments only on the affected tenant row or the shared catalog singleton; catalog invalidation is statement-level to avoid per-row catalog locks. Version tables retain RLS and grant `ceopro_app` SELECT only; trigger functions use a fixed search path and have PUBLIC execution revoked.

ENT-009 is **IMPLEMENTED / FOCUSED NATIVE TESTED**. This does not complete Phase 1B: ENT-007's separate native storage verification, ENT-008, ENT-002/U5-dependent behavior, ENT-010, ENT-011, CREDIT acceptance gaps, and any broader release verification remain tracked separately. No U1–U7 commercial decision was needed for this technical slice. No commit was created.

### Rollout, accounting, and remaining verification

Company-deletion and receipt-retention behavior remains under PRIVACY-001.

Existing authentication, tenant context, entitlement enforcement, connected-source authorization, ingestion, per-facet Credit metering, Data/AI Credit settlement, usage ledgers, provider-cost accounting and public response bodies were not redesigned. Provider-started failures conservatively enter `reconciliation_required`, so an operator reconciliation procedure is still needed before enabling production streams. ENT-008 implementation is complete for the authorized Stage 2 paths; release and rollout acceptance remains PARTIAL.

## Phase 2B promotion implementation gate — source reconciliation (2026-10-21)

Authority: original `TRACE-PROMO.md`, `ADR-PROMO-001-promotion-authority-redemption-and-stacking.md`, PROMO rows in the original acceptance specification, `02_ARCHITECTURE_SOURCE_PRECEDENCE.md`, original `DECISION_REGISTER.md`, and `OPEN_OWNER_DECISIONS.md`, read from the supplied Phase 2–6 ZIP. The current owner authorization permits this Phase 2B carve-out but does not resolve the explicitly pending O-PROMO terms or approve a migration.

Current Python promotion behavior is limited to Stripe-hosted Checkout `allow_promotion_codes=True` in `backend/src/ai/billing/service.py`. The existing code has no managed promotion CRUD/validation API, promotion-to-plan eligibility store, checkout-to-promotion reservation identity, durable redemption state, or invoice-settlement redemption/reconciliation path. The existing webhook event table deduplicates Stripe event IDs; `billing_payments` records invoice payment status. Neither table records promotion identity or supports a tenant-scoped reservation/cap, and checkout session IDs are returned to the caller rather than durably associated with a promotion. Therefore they cannot safely enforce PROMO-002/003 or prove final redemption under PROMO-001, including zero-total settled invoices. Existing Python billing/Stripe authority remains unchanged; no new code path or offer was activated.

| Original requirement | Current status | Dependency / no-guess boundary |
|---|---|---|
| PROMO-001 managed create/update/disable | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Needs durable promotion records and Stripe object identity. O-PROMO amount/type, duration, eligible plan/currency, dates, limits, invoice scope, and exact Stripe mapping remain pending. |
| PROMO-002 plan/date/currency/tenant eligibility | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Needs persisted terms and plan associations. No campaign terms or eligibility values may be inferred. |
| PROMO-003 concurrent tenant reservation, settled-invoice finalization and reconciliation | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Needs durable reservation/redemption records tied to checkout session, subscription, and initial invoice; unique provider session/invoice identity; and a concurrency-safe transaction. Finalize only on authoritative settled initial invoice, including valid zero-total; failed/unsettled/abandoned attempts remain non-final. |
| PROMO-004 Stripe monetary authority and no stacking with discounted custom offers | POLICY DIRECTION APPROVED; INTEGRATION NOT IMPLEMENTED | Python has no managed promotion/custom-offer checkout workflow to compose. The meaning/detection of a “discounted custom offer” and the affected checkout operation must be defined without broadly rejecting legitimate private contracts. |
| PROMO-005 customer validation feedback and platform management | NOT IMPLEMENTED | Requires authenticated Python API routes and audited Platform Owner authorization; frontend remains outside this batch. Do not leak internal provider costs or terms. |

### Migration 92 proposal — approval required before creation

No promotion-specific table exists in migrations 1–91. The smallest safe forward-only schema proposal is: (1) global `billing_promotions` with normalized unique code, lifecycle state, nullable-until-approved commercial/provider fields, and audit timestamps; (2) `billing_promotion_plans` associating a promotion with existing `usage_plans.plan_key` and only approved currency/term applicability; (3) `billing_promotion_redemptions` keyed to existing tenant/company, promotion, Stripe Checkout Session, subscription, and qualifying initial invoice, with provisional versus settled/released lifecycle and unique provider session/invoice identities; and (4) append-only actor/action audit through the existing `keel_platform_audit` authority. Enforce provider ID uniqueness, foreign-key safety, valid-term constraints, one redemption per provider session and qualifying invoice, and transactional per-promotion/per-tenant locking when checking whatever ceilings are later approved. Do not add a one-redemption-per-tenant constraint: O-PROMO leaves the per-tenant ceiling unspecified.

Global catalog rows should have RLS with `ceopro_app` read access only to approved active records and no direct mutation. Redemption rows should use ENABLE + FORCE RLS with tenant-scoped SELECT policy through `get_current_tenant()`; `ceopro_app` receives no direct INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE. Existing authenticated billing service and restricted Platform Owner API paths perform writes with tenant/actor revalidation and append-only audit; signed webhook processing reconciles the initial invoice under existing event/invoice locks. Provider actions use idempotency keys. Existing `billing_plan_prices`, `billing_subscriptions`, `billing_payments`, `billing_trials`, and customer rights remain untouched; no historical redemption backfill. Required verification includes fresh 1–92, populated 91→92, repeatability, restricted-role/RLS, concurrency, duplicate-event, zero-total settlement, and recovery tests.

This schema is only a proposal; **migration 92 was not created or applied**. O-PROMO must also provide actual values and eligibility terms before any record can be activated. The no-stacking direction is not enough to infer which existing `tenant_custom_contracts` count as discounted; those rows do not expose an approved universal discount classification. Do not disable current hosted promotion entry or make customer-impacting checkout changes while that classification is unresolved.

Existing regression checks: isolated offline `test_contract.py` + `test_safety.py` final run **42 passed**, one Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. This verifies the current checkout/idempotency, Stripe-hosted promotion parameter, signatures, auth, and safety contracts; it is not PROMO acceptance evidence. The first run found three stale plan-transition test doubles (`39 passed, 3 failed`) because they did not supply the newer SUB-005 custom-contract and rights readers. Test-only stubs were updated to describe a non-custom Stripe plan; rerun passed all 42. No billing service, Stripe, schema, frontend, price, customer rights, or accounting source was modified.

## Phase 2B implementation checkpoint — migration 92 and managed promotion backend (2026-10-21)

This entry updates the earlier Phase 2B source gate above. Original PROMO requirement IDs and assertions remain unchanged. Authority remains the original `TRACE-PROMO.md`, `ADR-PROMO-001-promotion-authority-redemption-and-stacking.md`, original PROMO acceptance rows, source-precedence document and decision register. The owner authorized the narrowly scoped migration 92 and policy-independent promotion infrastructure on 2026-10-21. The owner also explicitly selected tenant-deletion cascade for tenant promotion lifecycle records and actor anonymization (`actor_user_id = NULL`) when an individual actor is deleted; events remain present in the latter case.

Added `backend/src/infrastructure/database/migrations/20261028000400_keel_promotions.sql`, the 92nd migration after immutable migrations 1–91. It adds promotion definitions, links to existing plan keys, tenant-scoped reservations/redemptions, lifecycle events, provider/session/subscription/initial-invoice identities, term constraints, uniqueness/indexes, FORCE RLS, and restricted `ceopro_app` read-only grants. A composite `(tenant_id, redemption_id)` foreign key prevents lifecycle evidence from naming another tenant's redemption. Tenant deletion cascades the tenant's records; actor deletion anonymizes the lifecycle actor; direct lifecycle-event updates/deletes remain blocked apart from those FK actions. Existing `billing_payments` remains the payment authority; no second monetary or credit ledger exists.

Added `backend/src/ai/billing/promotions.py` for draft lifecycle, explicit term validation, eligibility, tenant reservations, configured-limit locking, checkout identity, uncertainty handling and settlement. Redemptions settle only for a verified paid `subscription_create` invoice with a matching existing succeeded `billing_payments` row; a valid zero-total invoice is accepted. Failed/unpaid invoices remain pending. Unrelated renewal invoices do not consume or reject a reservation. An expired Checkout releases its pending reservation. Duplicate settlement is idempotent. Managed-checkout retries reuse an already attached open session; a durable checkout-request claim and receipt-bound webhook recovery prevent an automatic second Stripe create after uncertain outcome.

Exact files changed in this checkpoint: `backend/src/ai/billing/promotions.py` (new), `backend/src/ai/billing/service.py`, `backend/src/ai/billing/stripe_api.py`, `backend/src/ai/billing/webhooks.py`, `backend/src/ai/core/authorization.py`, `backend/src/ai/routers/billing.py`, `backend/src/ai/routers/platform.py`, `backend/src/infrastructure/database/migrations/20261028000400_keel_promotions.sql` (new), `backend/tests/billing/test_promotions.py` (new), `backend/tests/billing/test_promotions_database.py` (new), and this ledger plus `docs/keel-integration/evidence/ACCEPTANCE_TEST_SPEC.csv`.

The existing Python checkout, signed webhook, invoice/payment, catalog, tenant membership and Platform Owner authorities are reused. Without a managed promotion code, legacy Stripe-hosted `allow_promotion_codes=True` behavior and its checkout idempotency key are unchanged. Managed checkout applies only the configured Stripe promotion reference and disables hosted code entry for that session. Stripe remains the monetary discount authority. Validation/customer and Platform Owner draft-management APIs are authenticated; draft create/update/disable actions use existing platform audit. No activation endpoint is present. `KEEL_MANAGED_PROMOTIONS_ENABLED` defaults off and rejects managed use when disabled. No real campaign, coupon or Stripe object was created or activated.

For custom/private contracts, the existing Python table does not provide an approved universal discounted-contract classification. Managed promotion eligibility therefore fails closed for private-contract tenants only; their current billing path is unchanged. Classification remains an O-CUSTOM/O-PROMO dependency for Phase 2C. O-PROMO still blocks actual campaign terms and activation. Frontend source/UI was outside this authorization.

## Phase 2C independent configurator slice (2026-10-21)

The Phase 2C handoff and original CUSTOM trace/acceptance rows were reviewed against current Python sources. The accepted policy directions are CUSTOM-001/CUSTOM-002 from the original ADRs; unresolved values remain O-ECON, O-MANUAL/U7, O-CATALOG/U5 and O-PROMO. No quote lifecycle schema was found in migrations 1-92: `tenant_custom_contracts` is one mutable row per tenant, `tenant_entitlement_overrides` stores only explicit nonnegative values, and `entitlement_audit_log` is append-only evidence, not a revisioned quote or acceptance authority. `keel_operation_receipts` is restricted to its existing source/competitor operation types. Therefore durable quote revisions, state transitions, quote-bound Stripe identities and safe offer notification cannot be implemented using current tables without a new migration.

Independent, non-persistent CUSTOM-001 support was added in `backend/src/ai/billing/custom_offers.py` and `GET /billing/custom-offers/configurator` plus `POST /billing/custom-offers/configuration/validate` in `backend/src/ai/routers/billing.py`. It reuses `billing.admin`'s established numeric contract-feature allowlist and reads canonical units/aggregation/reset metadata from `usage_features`; it exposes only the existing hourly/daily/weekly contract cadence values and the approved USD initial-checkout currency. Validation distinguishes omitted quantities from explicit zero, accepts only nonnegative integers, rejects unknown or catalog-unmapped codes, and rejects `None` as unlimited because the current override schema is `NOT NULL`. The response explicitly says it is not saved, does not calculate a price or change rights, and needs operator review. No quote, contract, price, Stripe, entitlement or accounting record is written. No feature dependency graph is present in Python, so required-feature dependencies remain unvalidated and unsupported rather than guessed.

Focused verification: `backend/tests/billing/test_custom_offers.py` — **11 passed** (unit tests plus HTTP route tests with injected tenant context and unauthenticated 401). Starlette/httpx deprecation and pytest-cache permission warnings occurred. Native RLS/catalog and browser verification were not run. The acceptance matrix updates only AT-CUSTOM-001 as PARTIAL; AT-CUSTOM-002–013 remain unchanged pending actual implementation or owner/schema gates.

## Phase 2C durable custom quote lifecycle (2026-10-21)

Owner approval authorized migration 93, `20261028000500_custom_offer_lifecycle.sql`, with exactly four tenant-scoped structures: `billing_custom_quotes`, immutable `billing_custom_quote_revisions`, append-only `billing_custom_quote_events`, and `billing_custom_quote_checkout_attempts`. The migration uses tenant-composite foreign keys, FORCE RLS, restricted direct table permissions (SELECT only for `ceopro_app`), and the narrow `billing_custom_quote_command` SECURITY DEFINER command with a hardened search path and authenticated Platform Owner / tenant-billing / verified-service context checks. Audit actor FKs are set NULL on individual actor deletion; tenant deletion cascades the quote lifecycle as approved. No historical rows were backfilled, and no retention timer or privacy-erasure workflow was introduced. Migrations 1–92 are unchanged.

The Python lifecycle in `backend/src/ai/billing/custom_offers.py` and routes in `backend/src/ai/routers/platform.py` and `backend/src/ai/routers/billing.py` support draft creation/read, immutable revisions, submit/approve, tenant-scoped accept/reject, expiry-on-accept, append-only history, and idempotent checkout-attempt identity preparation. Changed files in this batch are those three Python files, `backend/src/infrastructure/database/migrations/20261028000500_custom_offer_lifecycle.sql`, `backend/tests/billing/test_custom_quote_lifecycle_db.py`, this ledger, and `docs/keel-integration/evidence/ACCEPTANCE_TEST_SPEC.csv`. Acceptance returns `access_activated=false`; no plan assignment, subscription, payment, usage, entitlement, or Credit authority is changed. Checkout attempts are identity correlation only: no Stripe session is created and no payment/contract is activated. Operator/customer views separate private economic and FX snapshots. Secret-like keys are rejected recursively. The approved O-ECON, O-CATALOG/U5, U7, O-PROMO and CUSTOM-009 outbox gates remain in force; migration 93 does not implement quote economics, campaign terms, offer sending, price publication or activation.

A security review rejected relaxing the verified invoice-event quote-attempt metadata binding because it could correlate an unrelated invoice. The guard remains. Checkout-session events require quote-attempt metadata; a future separately approved checkout integration must preserve that identity through the qualifying invoice metadata. Tests use synthetic verified-event rows and zero-value billing payment rows only; they do not establish live Stripe signature or provider behavior.

Verification on the isolated `keel-custom-quote-93` PostgreSQL 17.11/pgvector project, database `keel_billing_test` on localhost 55442: fresh migrations 1–93 passed (93 migration files applied); repeat run applied 0. A separate disposable upgrade database applied migrations 1–92, seeded synthetic plan assignment, subscription, successful payment and Data/AI credit balances, then applied migration 93; exact row snapshots were unchanged, migration history contained 93 files, and a migration-runner repeat applied 0. Focused pytest run: `tests/billing/test_custom_quote_lifecycle_db.py` plus `tests/billing/test_custom_offers.py` — **16 passed** (5 native lifecycle tests and 11 existing unit/API tests). Warnings: one Starlette/httpx deprecation and two pytest-cache permission warnings. Native tests cover tenant isolation, restricted direct writes, authenticated lifecycle, concurrency/idempotency, revision immutability, actor/tenant deletion, no access activation, unchanged billing/credits, and checkout identity correlation against processed event and billing-payment records. No live Stripe, browser, or activation test ran.

Acceptance status: AT-CUSTOM-003 and AT-CUSTOM-010 are PARTIAL PASS because the durable lifecycle is implemented and focused-tested, including concurrent idempotent draft/revision requests, customer rejection and lazy expiry-on-accept. Concurrent edit-versus-approval, new lifecycle HTTP routes, actual provider recovery and the unapproved CUSTOM-009 send outbox are not verified/implemented. AT-CUSTOM-011 is PARTIAL PASS: acceptance-without-access and no accounting/entitlement side effects are verified; paid Stripe or authorized manual activation remains out of scope. AT-CUSTOM-001 remains PARTIAL for the separate read-only configurator/dependency map. AT-CUSTOM-002, AT-CUSTOM-004–008 remain blocked by O-ECON and other approved-term inputs. AT-CUSTOM-009 remains pending architecture approval. AT-CUSTOM-012 frontend/dashboard work and AT-CUSTOM-013 private catalog visibility remain unimplemented/unverified. This batch is not Phase 2C complete and does not authorize Phase 2D.

Baseline verified before editing: branch `integration/keel-full-migration`, HEAD `8e72f4b8739dbdc2bd017fa069b336e30a06a380`, clean worktree; migrations 1–93 unchanged. ## Phase 3A canonical import correctness batch (2026-10-21)

Baseline: `integration/keel-full-migration` at `1f57b75fe064cd4d7a2655a5681e7324bbf12d5d`; original Phase 3A requirements, IMPORT traceability, AT-IMPORT-001–012, IMPORT-001 ADR and source-precedence materials were read from the authorized architecture ZIP. The inspected upstream delta had no changes to extraction, canonical imports, products, inventory, connector ingestion or migrations; no upstream code was copied or integrated.

The Python import authorities remain `extraction/ingestion_pipeline.py`, `extraction/promotion.py`, `extraction/job_management.py`, and `routers/extraction.py`. The implementation preserves their accepted file/template parsing, validation, staged rows, savepoint-isolated promotion, tenant-filtered product queries, transaction/invoice/item mirrors, curated product prices, barcode fill-only behavior, usage reservation/accounting and response shape. Existing inventory, RAG, review, and connector paths were not replaced.

A demonstrated canonical identity defect was corrected in `backend/src/ai/extraction/promotion.py`: duplicate tenant product names and duplicate tenant barcode identities previously collapsed to whichever row a Python dictionary happened to retain. The promotion path now detects ambiguous existing name/barcode identities, conflicting imported barcode identities for the same normalized product name, and a unique exact name/barcode resolving to different tenant products. It writes the review reason into the existing staging row as `PARTIAL`, does not create/associate a canonical product for that row, and continues safe independent rows. It does not use fuzzy/name-similarity matching, override an existing curated barcode, or use a product from another tenant. The existing accepted case where a unique exact name matches while a different unclaimed incoming barcode is present remains unchanged.

A second demonstrated reporting defect was corrected in `backend/src/ai/routers/extraction.py`: an upload whose extraction/promotion left partial, failed, or incomplete rows was always finalized as `COMPLETED`. The final job state now uses the existing `PARTIAL` state and a bounded explanatory message for such outcomes; otherwise it remains `COMPLETED`. The response schema and accounting behavior are unchanged. The frontend was not changed; its status mapper still needs an explicit `PARTIAL` review presentation.

Changed files in this batch: `backend/src/ai/extraction/promotion.py`, `backend/src/ai/routers/extraction.py`, `backend/src/ai/tests/test_extraction_barcode.py`, `backend/src/ai/tests/test_extraction_promotion_integration_db.py`, new `backend/src/ai/tests/test_extraction_job_status.py`, `docs/keel-integration/evidence/ACCEPTANCE_TEST_SPEC.csv`, and this ledger. A second focused file-dispatch/typed-validation/catalog-matching suite passed **29/29**. The native import integration fixtures connected through the disposable database administrative URL. ## Phase 3B generic connector durability (2026-10-21)

Baseline: `integration/keel-full-migration` at `66438a590e9caef14cb8366c87ea70f8fd6775a1`. The approved CONNECT-001 through CONNECT-006 requirements and related IMPORT/ENT-008 requirements were read from the authoritative Phase 2-6 handoff. Migrations 1-93 remain unchanged; no new schema was necessary. Existing Python generic PostgreSQL/API connectors, onboarding and credential vault, operation receipts/lease fencing, and the Phase 3A staging pipeline remain the sole authorities.

This batch made these bounded corrections:

- Receipt-bound connector writes compare the current tenant source configuration and encrypted credential envelope against a deterministic fingerprint. The source row is locked `FOR SHARE` during the check and canonical transaction, serializing config/credential changes or disconnects against commit. The same check runs transactionally before provider work starts. Stale or disconnected sources cannot stage rows; the receipt-owned job is finalized as `FAILED` without disclosing credentials.
- `attach_ingestion_job` updates the in-memory receipt as well as its durable row so fenced recovery can find the exact job.
- Sync results with partial or failed rows no longer advance `last_synced_at` or report success. The persisted `ingestion_jobs` constraint allows only `QUEUED`, `PROCESSING`, `COMPLETED`, and `FAILED`; partial-quality connector runs therefore use the existing `FAILED` job state while retaining row-level counts and a nonsecret error description. No migration or new persisted status was added. Both DB and API schedulers now also fail the operation receipt and return an error result for partial/failed row outcomes instead of completing the receipt.
- Connector exception text is no longer written to job errors, logs, or worker results. Diagnostics retain exception class and safe HTTP/database codes without provider URLs or credential values.
- Native restricted-role tests cover forced RLS for staging/source/product visibility, tenant-local staging and explicit promotion with a same-named product in another tenant, credential rotation, and disconnect before provider start and before canonical commit.

No new registration API or provider was added. | Registration behavior was not expanded; source identity/classification ambiguities remain under U6/O-SOURCE. |

Existing scheduler, receipt identity, claims, lease recovery and independent DB/API source handling were reused; receipt/worker/onboarding native regressions passed. | Worker cadence/deployment, uncertain-provider reconciliation and live provider operation remain release gates. No retry interval was invented. |

Restricted-role staging and the existing explicit promotion routine were tested for tenant isolation and no cross-tenant product association. | Connector-to-canonical promotion depends on O-IMPORT identity/precedence and unresolved ownership/review behavior. Do not enable automatic promotion or claim full parity. |
| CONNECT-005 disconnect/reconnect | PARTIAL PASS. Native tests cover config/credential rotation and disconnect before provider start and before a fenced staging commit; stale work is rejected and job status remains truthful. | Full live-provider interruption/reconnect journeys and rollout monitoring remain Phase 6 gates. |

Final native connector, restricted-role/RLS, worker, onboarding/capacity, and receipt run: **51 passed, 3 warnings** (Starlette/httpx deprecation and short synthetic test JWT key). No external provider or production database was used. `git diff --check` passed. No migrations, billing rules, connected-source limits, entitlement mappings, or frontend contracts changed.

### Owner decision package for 4B/4C

| Decision | Already approved | Still unresolved and consequence | Safe recommendation (not implemented) | Blocked operation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| O-IMPORT - external product, variant, order and item identity (`IMPORT-001`; original `ADR-IMPORT-001` and Phase 4 canonical mapping) | Validated unambiguous records may promote; ambiguous conflicts are held for review. Preserve same-tenant composite identity, source provenance, curated/shared-product ownership; names alone never merge products. | Precedence among provider ID, SKU, barcode/GTIN, manual product identity and other stores; duplicate external keys across sources; variation-parent rules; order revision/refund identity and conflict review transitions. The current staging pipeline cannot supply durable cross-source identity on its own. | Start from provider+tenant+source scoped external IDs; never merge across sources by name; keep cross-source SKU/barcode/key collisions in review until an explicit mapping is approved. Define exact entity and revision keys per provider, not a global matcher. | Automatic product/variant association and writes; order/invoice/refund creation or correction; cross-store merge. |
| O-SOURCE - source ownership and canonical updates (`IMPORT-001`; `TRACE-STORE`; `PHASE4_COMPLETE_ARCHITECTURE.md` canonical mapping) | A source must not overwrite another authoritative source's inventory; preserve source-owned data and quarantine conflicts rather than applying last-write-wins. | Which source owns each product, price, stock/location and order field; whether a store can create/update/delete manual or shared records; conflict resolution and removal behavior. | Keep commerce fetches validated and staged; assign ownership explicitly per tenant/entity/field before any canonical write; preserve current values where two owners conflict. | Inventory/location synchronization, product/price updates, deletions and conflicting source reconciliation. |
| U6 / O-SOURCE - persistent source identity, counting, reconnect and access (`ENT-001`; approved ADR-ENT-001; ledger U6) | Count each active persistent connection once; one-time uploads are excluded; do not alter grandfathered access or change source-count rollout without impact assessment and separate approval. | How verified provider accounts map to existing `data_sources`, what constitutes reconnect vs duplicate, handling same shop represented multiple times, disconnected/disabled records, and whether classification changes affect a tenant's capacity. | Reuse an existing source only on a proven same-tenant/provider identity match; do not merge duplicate legacy rows or change current counts until an impact review and explicit U6 approval. | Store registration/reconnect when it would merge/reclassify/count existing identities or change access; deduplication/backfill/source-count cutover. Generic API/DB source rules remain in effect. |
| O-PROVIDER - provider contracts (Phase 4B/4C requirements and `PHASE4_DEPENDENCIES_AND_DECISIONS.md`) | Python remains the sole authority; the approved architecture requires provider verification, safe credentials, bounded sync and truthful failures. | Woo REST/API behavior, accepted statuses/refund allocation, Shopify API version/scopes/history access/include-test-orders, callback and delivery contracts, and sandbox credentials/permissions are not established for live execution by this package. | At the start of each provider phase, bind implementation to a verified current vendor contract and owner-supplied isolated sandbox; keep provider-specific work disabled until then. | Provider adapter behavior where contract details matter and real provider certification. This does not block reuse of the generic DB/API foundation. |

Migration decision: no migration was created. The generic lifecycle uses existing source, receipt, job and staging tables. Dedicated store identity/link uniqueness, provider page checkpoints, product/variant links, inventory locations and order/item links are not present. The historical Phase 4A migration proposal is not an authorization. Any minimal schema for these operations must be separately scoped, uniquely numbered after 93 in coordination with other pending migrations, FORCE-RLS/restricted-role reviewed, and approved before creation. Do not guess migration 94 or use existing JSON configuration as an identity/uniqueness substitute.

Phase 4B WooCommerce readiness: safe to begin only as a contract-and-fixture implementation slice after owner decisions needed by the specific operation are resolved. Public URL/SSRF transport can be prepared against verified behavior; connection activation, source identity/reconnect, product/order/inventory canonical writes and production certification remain gated by O-PROVIDER, U6/O-SOURCE and O-IMPORT. No live integration or automatic canonical write is ready now.


## Phase 4B WooCommerce provider adapter - 2026-10-21

Authority: supplied `KEEL_PHASES_2_TO_6_HANDOFF/phase-4/PHASE4_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `PHASE4_ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md`, `PHASE4_DEPENDENCIES_AND_DECISIONS.md`, and original `TRACE-WOO.md`; current Python source remains implementation evidence. No historical Node code was copied. The batch adds a reusable read-only Python REST client and raw-body signature verifier. It does not register or activate WooCommerce sources, connect to a live store, create webhooks, persist checkpoints, stage rows, or write canonical data.

### Provider contract checked against current official documentation

- The current recommended WooCommerce REST API is `/wp-json/wc/v3/`. HTTPS Basic Auth sends the consumer key and secret as username/password; key permissions derive from the associated WordPress user's capabilities. KEEL requires HTTPS and never falls back to query-string secrets. Sources: [REST API v3](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/v3/) and [Authentication](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/authentication).
- List endpoints default to 10 items and support 1-based `page` and `per_page`; official docs describe `Link`, `X-WP-Total`, and `X-WP-TotalPages`. The adapter follows the documented next link or total-page header, falls back to another page after a full page if totals are absent, and rejects repeated URLs/content and configured page/response bounds. Source: [REST API pagination](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/).
- Products and orders use REST v3. Variable products list variation IDs and variations use `/products/{product_id}/variations`; the adapter returns separate provider-scoped product and variation records, retaining the parent ID. Orders are returned as raw provider payloads; status/refund policy is not normalized. Sources: [Products](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/v3/products), [Product Variations](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/v3/product-variations/), [Orders](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/v3/orders/).
- WooCommerce webhook signatures are base64 HMAC-SHA256 over the raw payload in `X-WC-Webhook-Signature`; delivery metadata includes webhook and delivery IDs. The docs state unsuccessful deliveries are retried and the webhook is disabled after five consecutive failures by default. This batch implements only the constant-time signature helper: no HTTP receiver, durable replay record, source attribution or delivery acknowledgment. Source: [Webhooks](https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/apis/rest-api/v3/webhooks/).
- Core product docs expose stock fields, but do not establish a universal store-location contract. This adapter does not map inventory or location data. No official contract from the checked sources was used to invent an endpoint, API scope, status/refund policy, rate limit, or provider checkpoint.

### Implementation and evidence

Added `backend/src/market_scraper/woocommerce.py` with HTTPS URL validation, public DNS validation, per-request pinned TCP transport while preserving hostname TLS validation, redirect refusal, HTTPS Basic Auth, sanitized provider errors, bounded response/page traversal, products/variations/orders reads, and raw-body webhook HMAC verification. Records retain WooCommerce IDs and payloads; no canonical mapping is done. `backend/src/market_scraper/tests/test_woocommerce_adapter.py` covers URL/auth/SSRF/redirect behavior, REST paths, product/variation/order identity, pagination (including missing totals, repeated pages and configured bounds), sanitized 401/403/429/5xx responses, and signatures.

Focused command in the isolated Python 3.12 environment:
`pytest src/market_scraper/tests/test_woocommerce_adapter.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_connector_sync.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_api_connector_sync.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_connector_target_guard.py src/ai/tests/test_operation_receipts.py -q -p no:cacheprovider`
Result: **41 passed**. These are unit tests with HTTPX mock transports plus existing generic connector/receipt regressions. No PostgreSQL migration, native RLS, live store, webhook endpoint, scheduler, or canonical staging test ran. No new dependency manifest entry was added; HTTPX already exists in `market_scraper/requirements.txt`, and its installed httpcore transport backend is used for DNS pinning. Live-provider certification remains a Phase 6 gate.

No database changes were needed for this isolated reader/helper. The current `data_sources`, generic operation receipts, jobs and staging schema do not provide a verified Woo shop identity binding, durable provider cursor/checkpoint, webhook delivery replay identity/source attribution, or unique provider product/order mapping. Those capabilities must not be simulated in JSON configuration or represented as complete synchronization. No migration was created; any future durable identity/checkpoint/webhook schema needs a separate narrow proposal and migration approval coordinated after migration 93.

Registration endpoint remains absent. |

Only product-read access is confirmed by this method. | No credential-vault registration/rotation, shop identity/currency/scope attestation, tenant capacity binding or live store. U6/O-SOURCE and provider permissions apply. |

REST v3 products/orders and variation paths use page/per_page, Link/total-page pagination, missing-total fallback, repeated-content rejection and configurable page/byte bounds; 429, timeout and 5xx are errors (retryable classification only). | No provider-specific durable checkpoints, modified-time overlap, retry/backoff schedule, equal-timestamp recovery, live rate-limit behavior or receipt-bound worker integration. No watermark is advanced here. |

O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE/U6 gates remain. |

Requires schema/identity decisions and separate migration approval as applicable. |

### Actionable identity/ownership decisions for 4B/4C

| Decision | Approved rule | Unresolved choice and recommendation | Blocked operation |
|---|---|---|---|
| O-IMPORT: product/variation identity | IMPORT-001 allows only validated, unambiguous matches; names alone never merge products. Preserve tenant/source provenance. | Decide whether canonical mapping starts from `(tenant, source, Woo product ID)` and `(tenant, source, Woo variation ID)`; separately decide whether/how SKU, barcode/GTIN or mapped parent can link across stores. Safe recommendation: use source-scoped IDs as external identity and hold all cross-store/key collisions for explicit review. | Product/variation association, cross-store merge, or canonical product/price writes. |
| O-IMPORT: order/refund identity | Preserve distinct legitimate sales; do not silently duplicate revenue or inventory; ambiguous rows are held. | Define order revision identity, line-item identity, refund-line allocation and treatment of amount-only refunds. Safe recommendation: source-scoped order/item IDs, idempotent revisions; quarantine refunds without line allocation. | Canonical transaction/invoice/item creation, correction, refund or cancellation effects. |
| O-SOURCE: inventory and fields | Source-owned inventory is preserved; no arbitrary last-write-wins. | Assign ownership per field and warehouse/location, including stock, price, deletion and reconnect changes. Safe recommendation: retain staged provider values until explicit tenant/entity/field ownership exists. | Inventory/location writes, product price changes and source-driven delete/deactivation. |
| U6/O-SOURCE: store registration, reconnect and counting | Active persistent connections count once; one-time uploads do not count; access-changing rollout requires impact assessment and separate approval. | Decide verified shop identity key, duplicate/reconnect matching, disabled/disconnected rows, and whether duplicate records merge/count differently. Safe recommendation: no automatic merge/reclassification or change in current capacity counts absent verified identity and impact review. | Tenant-facing store registration/reconnect/deduplication where it changes source counts or rights. |

No WooCommerce implementation exists upstream in that comparison. No upstream changes were copied.

No production route or worker activates this module. No canonical product, variation, inventory, order, invoice or refund writes are enabled. No API/frontend contract, migration, billing, usage, Credits or source-capacity behavior changed. Phase 4C is not started; Shopify can reuse the secured read-only boundary pattern, but shared durable registration/webhook/cursor infrastructure and the stated identity/ownership decisions remain prerequisites before a production sync path.


## Phase 4C Shopify read foundation and privacy verification - 2026-10-21

Authority: supplied Phase 4C `SHOP-001`?`SHOP-008` requirements, acceptance criteria, source-precedence and cross-phase decisions, and `ADR-PRIVACY-001-provider-privacy-and-retained-business-records.md`. The current Python repository remains implementation evidence. This batch adds a reusable, read-only Shopify GraphQL client and pure webhook/privacy verification helpers. It does not add OAuth routes, register a store, persist credentials/events, acknowledge deliveries, stage or promote records, run privacy jobs, or connect to a live merchant.

### Verified Shopify provider contract

- The currently latest stable GraphQL Admin API version on 2026-10-21 is `2026-10`; stable versions `2026-01`, `2026-04`, `2026-07`, and `2026-10` were supported. Requests use a pinned version URL, POST JSON, and `X-Shopify-Access-Token`. The adapter refuses `latest`, unsupported versions, redirects, non-public resolved addresses, and versions Shopify reports as fallen-forward. [API versioning](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/usage/versioning), [GraphQL Admin API](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest)
- `currentAppInstallation.accessScopes` and `shop.myshopifyDomain` are queried for provider-side verification. The adapter checks scopes before each product, order, or inventory read and confirms that Shopify returned the requested shop identity. [Manage access scopes](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/authentication-authorization/manage-access-scopes), [currentAppInstallation](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/queries/currentappinstallation)
- The adapter uses read-only products/variants, orders/line items, and location/inventory-level GraphQL queries and preserves Shopify GraphQL IDs and raw money/status values. It requires `read_products`, `read_orders`, or `read_inventory` respectively. Shopify's default order access is the most recent 60 days; `read_all_orders` is surfaced as a distinct granted scope and is not requested or assumed by the client. [Products](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/objects/Product), [variants](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/objects/ProductVariant), [orders](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/objects/Order), [locations](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/objects/Location), [inventory](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/admin-graphql/latest/objects/InventoryLevel), [access scopes](https://shopify.dev/docs/api/usage/access-scopes)
- GraphQL partial errors are rejected without returning partial data. Cursor traversal detects repeated/non-progressing cursors, enforces page and response bounds, and retries only read-only queries within configured attempt/delay bounds using GraphQL cost status or HTTP `Retry-After`. [GraphQL rate limits](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/apis/graphql-admin/rate-limits)
- Shopify signs raw webhook bytes using base64 HMAC-SHA256; `X-Shopify-Webhook-Id` identifies an individual delivery and `X-Shopify-Event-Id` correlates deliveries from the same merchant action. The helper verifies HMAC in constant time, allowlists topics, compares expected shop/domain and an optional known shop ID, validates bounded JSON, and extracts minimal non-PII privacy request identifiers. Shopify requires public App Store apps to subscribe to `customers/data_request`, `customers/redact`, and `shop/redact`, acknowledge requests with a 2xx response, and complete the action within 30 days unless lawful retention prevents it. Failed HTTPS webhook deliveries are retried up to eight times over four hours; repeated failures may remove an Admin-API-managed subscription. This code is only a signature/parser helper, not a receiver or replay defense; it does not acknowledge or process an event without durable receipt persistence. [Verify webhook deliveries](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/webhooks/verify-deliveries), [webhooks](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/webhooks), [privacy compliance](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/compliance/privacy-law-compliance)

Shopify access token behavior differs by app type. Offline tokens are the default for background work; expiring public-app offline tokens last one hour and rotate refresh tokens (refresh-token lifetime is initially 90 days); custom-app tokens may be non-expiring. Public apps must use expiring offline tokens for GraphQL by 2027-01-01. Since O-PROVIDER has not selected the KEEL app distribution model or authorization flow, no OAuth exchange, refresh-token rotation, token lifecycle, or custom/public-app assumptions were implemented. The adapter accepts a short-lived token argument and uses it only in an HTTPS request header; caller integration must load/decrypt it through the existing Python credential vault. [Access tokens](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/authentication-authorization/access-tokens), [expiring offline migration](https://shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/authentication-authorization/migrate-to-expiring-offline-access-tokens)

### Changed files and verification

- `backend/src/market_scraper/shopify.py`: new read-only, HTTPS/DNS-pinned GraphQL client; explicit stable version validation; current provider shop/scope verification; product/variant, order/line-item, and inventory/location reads; bounded cursor traversal, response sizes and safe read retries; sanitized provider errors; raw-body webhook HMAC and minimal privacy-event identity helpers. No OAuth, persistence, endpoint, credential vault write, queue, tenant-source binding, canonical promotion, accounting, or privacy mutation.
- `backend/src/market_scraper/tests/test_shopify_adapter.py`: mock-transport coverage for version/domain/HTTPS, provider token rejection and scopes, shop identity, products/variants and nested pagination, orders/line items and money identity, inventory scope/location identity, GraphQL partial errors and version mismatch, throttle retries, cursor nonprogress, raw-body HMAC/topic/shop/delivery checks, and safe minimal privacy parsing.

Executed in isolated Python 3.12 environment (real installed HTTPX; no dependencies installed or manifests changed):

`pytest src/market_scraper/tests/test_shopify_adapter.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_woocommerce_adapter.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_connector_sync.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_api_connector_sync.py src/market_scraper/tests/test_connector_target_guard.py src/ai/tests/test_operation_receipts.py -q -p no:cacheprovider`

Result: **56 passed**. Tests used HTTPX mock transports and existing unit regressions. No native PostgreSQL/RLS, OAuth, live shop, public/custom app, external webhook delivery, token refresh, durable replay, scheduled sync, privacy persistence/deletion, staging, canonical write, or provider sandbox test ran. No migration was created or applied; migrations 1–93 are unchanged. No frontend/API route, billing, usage, Credit, source-capacity, or canonical data changed.

### SHOP-001–008 status

| SHOP-001 OAuth install state | BLOCKED | None; safe shop-domain parser only. | O-PROVIDER must select public/custom distribution and authorization model; durable nonce/state and Python/frontend callback contract are absent. |

| SHOP-004 GraphQL reads/pagination | PARTIAL | Provider reads for products/variants, orders/line items and inventory/locations; bounds, nested cursors, throttling and GraphQL errors focused-tested. | Not integrated into scheduled worker, provider checkpoint or staging. O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE gate canonical promotion. |
| SHOP-005 order reconciliation | PARTIAL | Raw order and line-item identity, money, test/cancel/status fields preserved. | No refund allocation, status normalization, order revision identity, staging, invoice mapping or canonical writes. O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE. |
| SHOP-006 signed webhook/replay | PARTIAL | Raw-body HMAC, allowed topics, expected shop header/body check, optional known shop ID, delivery ID/event ID metadata and body bound. | No receiver, durable event idempotency/replay state, shop-to-tenant attribution, or durable schedule signal. |

Legal/operational field map and irreversible-action sign-off remain required by PRIVACY-001. |

### Migration and operational integration proposal (not approved or created)

Migrations 1–93 have generic data sources, encrypted credential envelopes, operation receipts, sync jobs, and import staging, but lack durable Shopify shop identity binding, one-use OAuth state, refresh-token rotation fencing/versioning, provider stream checkpoints, unique provider delivery replay records, and privacy request/re-import fences. Do not overload generic JSON configuration, Stripe webhook records, or competitor/source-only receipts for these functions.

A future smallest coherent forward-only schema proposal should coordinate a migration identifier only after outstanding Phase 2D/Phase 3A work is reconciled; do not assume migration 94 or the historical `20261025001000_shopify_oauth_events_privacy.sql` proposal is available. Candidate tenant-scoped, composite-FK/`FORCE RLS` structures: (1) provider installation/source identity + encrypted-token version/refresh fencing + authorization-state nonce hashes; (2) stream cursor/checkpoint state linked to an authorized source; (3) unique provider webhook delivery receipt with source/tenant attribution and explicit received/processing/reconciliation states; and (4) minimal privacy request receipt/fence keyed by provider shop and request identity, with no raw payload/PII. Restricted-role write methods, actor/service context, audit behavior, deletion implications, and exact U6 identity uniqueness must be separately designed and approved. Until then, no shop registration, OAuth callback, webhook acknowledgment, privacy job, source activation, or sync scheduling is enabled.

### Owner gates and Phase 5A independence

- **O-PROVIDER:** choose public App Store vs custom app and authorization/token lifecycle. This blocks OAuth callback, installation, refresh/reconnect, and production credential rotation. Safe recommendation: select the app distribution model first, then implement exactly that flow.
- **U6/O-SOURCE:** define durable shop-to-tenant/source identity, duplicate/reconnect behavior, capacity accounting and uninstall effects. This blocks source registration, reconnect, uninstall state changes, and scheduling.
- **O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE:** define product/variant/order/refund cross-source identity and field/inventory ownership. This blocks cross-source matches, product/order/invoice/refund/inventory canonical writes. Current adapter emits only raw provider-scoped records.
- **PRIVACY-001 legal/operational gates:** decide jurisdictions, covered fields, lawful retention/exception logic, report/exclusion requirements, and authority for irreversible erasure. This blocks privacy job execution and any completion claim; signed request authentication can be tested in isolation.
- Shopify frontend authorization/status screens remain separate approved work; no frontend changes were made.

Phase 5A pricing orchestration can proceed independently only if it uses existing Python catalog/pricing authority and does not depend on Shopify data or unresolved source ownership. Real end-to-end Shopify sync remains blocked by authorization model and durable source/webhook/checkpoint/privacy persistence, plus O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE for canonical promotion.

No Shopify, OAuth, webhook, migration, privacy, connector-worker, or credential-vault implementation appeared in the upstream-only diff. `bf0b929` updates `backend/src/market_scraper/requirements.txt` for Pillow CVE remediation; it is a shared market-scraper dependency file but does not overlap this adapter (which uses existing HTTPX). Other upstream changes concern marketing/RAG, plan-catalog testing, workflow configuration, pending actions, and frontend lockfile. No teammate changes were integrated or copied.


## Phase 5A pricing intelligence implementation and verification - 2026-10-21

Authority: owner authorization for the bounded Phase 5A batch; original `PRICE-001` through `PRICE-008` acceptance rows; approved `ADR-PRICE-001-pricing-evidence-freshness-and-fallback.md`; Phase 5 requirements and dependency/decision matrix. The original acceptance assertions remain unchanged. A narrow policy-independent production correction was added: generated pricing confidence is validated before evidence persistence. No scoring algorithm, router, migration, frontend, entitlement, usage, credit, or catalog source was otherwise changed.

### Existing Python capabilities reused and verified

- `backend/src/ai/routers/pricing.py` exposes authenticated single and bulk recommendation routes using `TenantContext`; the recommendation pipeline reads the product using tenant and product IDs and returns a non-disclosing 404 when it is absent from that tenant. The existing frontend supplies `product_id` as a query parameter.
- `backend/src/ai/pricing/data_access.py` reads only same-tenant products and observations joined through tenant-scoped competitor mappings. It limits the local market set to exact, ALLOWED, available prices and active mappings/tracked competitors, and currently chooses the latest *qualifying* record. Python historical fallback and the existing configured 1095-day default remain unchanged.
- `backend/src/ai/pricing/pipeline.py` preserves the existing comparable/regional/internal fallback hierarchy, reference-only cross-currency note, guardrails, demand/stock holds, explanation, and recommendation persistence. Fallback basis is explicitly returned in `data_basis` and explanation; the response does not update the product's current price.
- `backend/src/ai/pricing/evidence.py` reuses the shared evidence writer and links `recommendation_outcomes` to evidence. `pipeline.py` now rejects non-finite or out-of-range generated confidence before persistence; this preserves the existing scorer output and prevents invalid recommendations from being saved. Migration inspection confirms evidence category and non-forecast shape constraints, but no database confidence-score range check.

### Focused verification actually executed

All Python checks used the existing isolated Python 3.12 environment and installed real project dependencies:

This is needed because receipt operation/target constraints in migrations 90 and 91 exclude pricing and current evidence/outcome records do not carry request idempotency identity. A new forward migration must be coordinated with pending migration proposals; no identifier, SQL, or application was assumed.
- **PRICE-007/008:** any evidence confidence-bound constraint or recommendation history/UI extension needs its own approved acceptance contract. No frontend or shared evidence writer was altered here.

### O-MPI decision package ? owner response required

The governing source is approved `ADR-MPI-001` and its original requirements MPI-004/MPI-006/MPI-009/MPI-010. The owner has already selected the direction: unreliable numeric scores must be suppressed as UNKNOWN/null. The values and per-scope rules below remain open; they must not be inferred from Python defaults or the Node reference implementation.

| Decision | Current Python behavior | Choices for approval and consequences | Safe proposal (not approved) | Blocked behavior |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Minimum qualified sample per scope | `MPI_MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE` currently defaults to 5; low sample currently returns numeric MPI plus `LOW_SAMPLE_SIZE`. | One common threshold makes scopes comparable but may suppress sparse scopes; separate BUSINESS/PRODUCT/COMPETITOR thresholds fit source availability but yield different disclosure points. Higher values hide more numeric results; lower values expose more. | Select and publish an explicit threshold for each scope (or explicitly choose one shared value), independent of code defaults. | Applying selected UNKNOWN/null suppression to low-sample numeric responses. |
| Sampling cap and order | Current MPI loads every eligible scored review, ordered newest first; scoring math already applies its existing recency and volume weights. | No hard cap uses all rows; fixed cap bounds work but changes which reviews can affect results; per-scope caps vary effective coverage. Choose deterministic ordering/tie behavior if capped. | Decide whether there is a cap and its scope-specific value/order; do not treat the historical 100-row reference as approved. | Bounded sample completeness/performance contract and any score based on truncated samples. |
| Freshness / stale reviews | Current algorithm applies Python recency weighting; the ingestion query now excludes future timestamps but has no age cutoff. | Recency weights alone preserve older signal; a hard age window excludes old reviews; per-scope windows can produce different eligible histories. Any cutoff changes historic samples and scores. | Keep existing recency weighting until an explicit per-scope age window is selected. | Excluding older reviews or asserting freshness-qualified MPI results. |
| Qualified source types | Current query requires source `ALLOWED` and safety `SAFE`; existing reliability weights rank `PUBLIC_API`, `PUBLIC_FEED`, `MANUAL`, and unknown methods use the existing default. | Accept all explicitly allowed/safe rows, define an allowlist by method, or define scope-specific source eligibility. Narrower eligibility reduces sample size and may suppress more scopes. | Approve an explicit source/method allowlist per scope; continue excluding quarantined/blocked content. | New source allowlist/eligibility rules and claims that a source mix is reliable. |
| Per-scope reliability and suppression result | Existing reliability weights and cold-start status are shared; zero usable reviews already return UNKNOWN; low-sample numeric remains. | Global reliability rules are simpler; scope-specific rules reflect different provenance. Keep response shape and put `mpi: null` with `status: UNKNOWN`, or add a separately versioned typed envelope; changing status semantics affects clients. | Choose scope-specific reliability inputs and use UNKNOWN/null while retaining existing outer fields, subject to an API compatibility check. | Publishing/suppressing numeric scores under the full selected MPI-001 direction and any frontend display contract. |

No decision is requested to reopen the approved MPI-001 direction. These choices only fill its remaining numeric/source parameters.
