## Owner-approved source identity, inventory ownership, reconnect, and privacy foundation — 2026-10-21

The owner explicitly approved the following implementation directions on 2026-10-21; these approvals supplement, and do not rewrite, the original architecture/ADR history:

- **O-IMPORT / IMPORT-001:** provider IDs identify records only inside a verified tenant + provider + store/source + entity-type namespace. Products/variants from different stores are never silently merged. Names, SKUs, barcodes and similar attributes are candidates only; cross-source association requires explicit auditable review. Ambiguous/conflicting rows stay staged or held. Existing canonical IDs and lineage stay intact. This does not define refund or order-revision semantics.
- **O-SOURCE / IMPORT-001, IMPORT-006, STORE-005/006, WOO-004, SHOP-004:** inventory observations remain tied to their source and location. One source cannot replace another source's values or manual inventory; preserve conflicting observations and hold canonical updates until a tenant/product/location authority is explicitly selected. No sum/latest/max rule, product/location deletion, or historical inventory deletion is inferred.
- **U6 / ENT-001, CONNECT-001/005, STORE-002/003/007, WOO-006, SHOP-002/007:** verify tenant/provider/store identity before new-versus-reconnect classification. A verified reconnect reuses its logical source identity; duplicates of the same verified identity are prevented. Existing capacity rules remain. CSV uploads remain excluded. Ambiguous legacy sources are not merged, deleted, reclassified, backfilled, or recounted; produce an access-impact report before any such cleanup. No new entitlement is granted.
- **PRIVACY-001 / SHOP-006/007/008:** implement authenticated, attributed, durable, idempotent privacy-request receipt and review state with minimal nonsecret evidence; do not claim completion on receipt. Apply a reimport fence only when affected identity and scope are established safely. Irreversible customer/tenant deletion, financial-record erasure, legal retention periods, jurisdiction assumptions, field-level erasure rules, and destructive processing remain unauthorized pending their specific legal/operational approval.

These are approved directions, not implementation evidence. This bounded pass found no independently deployable production correction: the current schema cannot persist verified provider-store identity, external entity/review identity, per-source/location inventory observations, provider delivery replay identity, or privacy request/fence state. The existing WooCommerce and Shopify adapters preserve provider IDs in returned records, and generic `data_sources`/`ingestion_jobs`/`import_staging_rows` retain tenant/source/job staging lineage; those structures do not provide the approved cross-request identity uniqueness, reviewed association, durable checkpoints/delivery deduplication, or privacy receipt/fence. No code, schema, test, customer-data operation, or production activation was performed. The existing staging and read adapters remain unchanged; this approval does not make staged records canonical.

### First coordinated migration scope for separate approval (proposal only)

No migration identifier is reserved. The labels below are dependency-order placeholders after migration 93, not filenames, migrations, or permission to create/apply DDL. Reconcile the sequence with the pending Phase 2D notification and Phase 3A typed-import proposals before assigning identifiers. Keep migrations 1–93 immutable.

**A. Verified provider source lifecycle (first schema approval candidate; STORE-001/002/003/008, CONNECT-001/003/005, WOO-002/003/006, SHOP-002/003/006/007).** Reuse `data_sources`, encrypted `connection_credentials_vault`, `ingestion_jobs`, `keel_operation_receipts`, and existing tenant context. Add a provider binding keyed by `(tenant_id, source_id)` with verified provider kind, provider-normalized verified store key, verification state/time and nonsecret capability/version evidence; composite FK to the existing source. Add a uniqueness guard for `(tenant_id, provider, verified_store_key)` across the logical source lifecycle so reconnect resolves to the existing source even when inactive; do not backfill or merge legacy sources. Add per-source/per-stream checkpoint rows with opaque bounded cursor, version/fence and update time. Add provider delivery receipts with source attribution, provider delivery/event identity, topic, body digest, receipt state and timestamps; unique delivery identity within provider/store namespace, with no raw payload, access token, webhook secret or PII. Use composite tenant FKs, `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, tenant policies and restricted direct access (SELECT only where compatible); narrowly scoped audited commands must verify trusted transaction-local tenant/principal/service context, source ownership/active state and receipt/lease fences. These records do not alter `data_sources.is_active` or capacity until a verified registration/reconnect operation is authorized. Fresh install creates only empty structures. Upgrade leaves all existing rows and entitlement counts unchanged; no legacy backfill. Forward correction is additive; rollback is forward-only after writes because bindings/checkpoints/events are durable evidence. Tests: fresh and populated-upgrade/repeatability, duplicate/reconnect concurrency, capacity unchanged, cross-tenant RLS, restricted-role command denial/authorization, credential non-disclosure, disconnect/credential-version/lease fencing, duplicate/out-of-order provider delivery, checkpoint monotonicity, no early webhook acknowledgment.

Provider normalization and authentication still depend on O-PROVIDER: this schema does not decide Woo store identity normalization, Shopify public/custom app model, OAuth/token refresh, scopes, API versions, or live webhook registration. A provider binding can be created only from a provider-verified identity using a separately approved provider flow.

**B. Provider external identity and reviewed reconciliation (IMPORT-003/009/010, CONNECT-004, STORE-005/007, WOO-004/005, SHOP-004/005).** Reuse `products`, `transactions`, `invoices`, `invoice_items`, `import_jobs`/`ingestion_jobs`, `import_staging_rows`, and existing promotion/review behavior. Add a source-scoped external-entity identity keyed by tenant/source/provider/entity type/provider record ID, with optional provider-parent identity, staging-row link and optional same-tenant canonical target; include explicit reconciliation state and conflict reason. Enforce unique provider identity per source namespace, not globally across stores. Add append-only reviewed-association events referencing identity, staging row, same-tenant canonical target and authenticated reviewer/decision. Composite tenant FKs must cover `data_sources`, staging rows, products, transactions/invoices where existing keys support them; `FORCE RLS`, restricted read/write functions and auditable explicit association are required. No automatic cross-source merge, order refund/revision interpretation, or canonical write is implied. Fresh install adds empty structures; populated upgrade preserves source and canonical rows without inferred mappings/backfill. If a source reference cannot be expressed by existing `data_sources` (e.g. typed upload identity), resolve that in the typed-import schema before applying this dependency. Tests: two stores sharing IDs/SKUs remain separate, tenant mismatch rejected, candidate attributes do not auto-associate, reviewed association is auditable/idempotent, ambiguous rows stay held, unrelated valid rows remain promotable, duplicate/replay identity protection, restricted-role RLS.

**C. Source/location inventory observations and explicit authority selection (IMPORT-006, STORE-006, WOO-004, SHOP-004).** Reuse `inventory` as the existing canonical quantity authority, plus provider identity from B. Add provider location identity linked to tenant/source and append-only inventory observations keyed by tenant/source/product identity/location/provider observation identity, retaining quantity, provider-observed time and safe evidence digest/status. Add explicit tenant/product/location authority assignment with actor and audit event only when a tenant-authorized owner selects a source. Conflicting source/manual observations remain separately queryable and held; only a matching explicit authority may propose/update the existing canonical `inventory` row in a fenced transaction. No aggregation, last-write-wins, product/location deletion, or historical stock backfill. Composite FKs and `FORCE RLS`; restricted role reads via tenant policy and writes only through audited source-observation/review/authority commands. Fresh/upgrade preserve current inventory unchanged. Tests: same product/location observations from two stores persist separately, manual inventory is never silently replaced, conflicts remain held, selected-source write is fenced/idempotent, cross-tenant/source denial, source product/location removal does not erase history.

**D. Minimal Shopify privacy receipt and reimport fence (PRIVACY-001, SHOP-006/007/008; depends on A).** Reuse provider delivery receipts from A and current raw-body HMAC verifier. Add a tenant/source/shop-attributed privacy request identity and review/action state keyed to verified provider request/delivery identity, plus a fence only for a provider subject identity/scope that can be safely established. Store no raw webhook body, credential, webhook secret, unnecessary personal data, or unencrypted subject identifier. Restricted role cannot mark a request complete through a direct table write; state transitions must be audited and cannot claim erasure/compliance merely from receipt. Tenant-safe composite FKs, `FORCE RLS`, and authorized, narrowly scoped commands apply. Fresh/upgrade preserve all customer/business data; no deletion or historical backfill. Tests: signed attribution, duplicate request idempotency, cross-shop/tenant rejection, review visibility authorization, fence blocks only matching re-import scope, invalid/unknown scope remains review-required, no destructive side effects and no false completion. The owner-approved safe receipt does not approve field maps, jurisdiction, retention, erasure, financial-record handling, or irreversible action. If safe reimport fencing requires an approved subject-key/HMAC lifecycle or field/scope mapping not present in current authorities, split that fence extension and leave destructive processing disabled.

The migration dependency order is A → B → C → D (with the caveat that D's basic signed receipt may reuse A, while any subject fence follows its approved field/key design). Typed immutable invoice/inventory drafts (IMPORT-004/007/012) remain a separate schema decision and must be coordinated before any B rows refer to upload staging semantics. The Phase 2D notification outbox, immutable catalog history, pricing/MPI operation history, and platform financial-operation schema are unrelated migration proposals and are not folded into these migrations. For every migration, inspect current schema/trigger/functions first, test fresh and populated upgrades plus repeatability on disposable PostgreSQL 17, verify `ceopro_app` grants/RLS and seeded-record preservation, and do not touch shared/production databases.

### Current status and next approval boundary

O-IMPORT, O-SOURCE, U6, and the technical receipt/review portion of PRIVACY-001 are approved as recorded above; no prior decisions or architecture history were rewritten. Remaining gates are provider-specific O-PROVIDER; privacy field/jurisdiction/retention/irreversible-action rules; order/refund/revision contracts; any access-changing legacy classification (requires an impact report and separate authorization); and schema/security approval. Until schema approval, no persistent provider identity, registration/reconnect binding, durable checkpoint/webhook delivery, cross-source association, source-owned inventory observation/authority, or privacy receipt/fence path is implemented or enabled. No migration was created or applied, no tests ran in this documentation/proposal pass, and no local production commit was made. `origin/main` could not be refreshed: the last local SHA is `c8c4b77ae5a9ec7a77ed105256fa5316aa09fa90`, merge base `81ac008d6f1288ec4bbf9c8996bc80222f76ef2c`, nine upstream-only commits; `git ls-remote` failed because the configured proxy at `127.0.0.1:9` was unreachable. The known diff contains no source identity/store/ingestion/privacy/migration overlap; `bf0b929` changes the shared market-scraper Pillow dependency only. Recheck remote before implementation.

## Provider lifecycle migration 94 and implementation — 2026-10-21

The owner approved the four foundational directions on 2026-10-21 and separately authorized the first provider-lifecycle migration. Migration `20261028000600_provider_lifecycle.sql` is implemented as the next chronological migration after 93. Migrations 1–93 are unchanged.

### Schema and behavior delivered

- `keel_provider_sources` binds an existing tenant `data_sources` row to a verified provider/store identity. A tenant/provider/store unique constraint prevents duplicate logical connections. Shopify binding requires the verified `shop_id` GID and canonical `myshopify.com` domain returned by the authenticated GraphQL shop check. The WooCommerce adapter verifies product-read access but does not return a stable provider account ID, so its store binding fails closed.
- `keel_provider_sync_checkpoints` stores a bounded cursor/page/modified-time checkpoint for a verified source. Its SECURITY DEFINER function verifies tenant context, completed ingestion job, source state/version, matching ENT-008 source receipt, provider-started state, active lease and generation. Repeating the same receipt/checkpoint is idempotent; stale or partial work cannot advance it. `finish_ingestion_job` can invoke advancement in the same transaction as the completed job update. Existing generic and store provider workers do not yet pass provider checkpoints, so this is verified infrastructure, not an end-to-end store scheduler.
- `keel_provider_delivery_receipts` stores authenticated provider delivery identity, SHA-256 of the raw body, event type and allowlisted metadata. Direct runtime table writes are revoked; narrowly scoped functions record and transition deliveries. Duplicate IDs return the existing receipt only when type and body digest match; a conflicting replay is rejected. Shopify's existing raw-body HMAC/shop verifier is connected to durable receipt recording. No raw payload, customer fields, token or credential is stored.
- All three tables use tenant-safe composite keys/FKs, FORCE RLS and SELECT-only direct `ceopro_app` grants. Binding, checkpoint and delivery changes use hardened, explicitly granted functions. Binding requires an active tenant owner/admin and records a nonsecret event in the existing audit log.
- Credential/config changes or source deactivation advance provider versions and fence the binding as `verification_required` or `disabled`. Reverification reconnects the same source identity. The `data_sources` row remains the connected-source quota authority; binding does not add a source, alter capacity counts, or reclassify legacy records.
- Company and source foreign keys use RESTRICT. Per the owner's explicit “Block deletion” choice, hard tenant/source deletion is blocked while provider lifecycle evidence remains. Existing soft disconnect and credential removal remain supported. This is not a retention or erasure policy; a future approved privacy workflow is still required.

The migration adds only a composite unique index on existing operation receipt `(tenant_id, receipt_id)` so PostgreSQL can enforce the checkpoint's tenant-safe receipt FK. It does not add provider product/order maps, inventory locations, typed drafts, privacy request tables/fences, notification outboxes, or accounting structures. No backfill occurred.

### Verification evidence

- Fresh disposable PostgreSQL 17/pgvector run: Final_schema plus migrations 1–94 applied successfully; rerun applied **0** migrations.
- Populated disposable 93→94 upgrade: 93 prior migrations present; existing source row remained 1→1, tenant membership remained 1→1, provider binding backfill remained 0; rerun applied **0** migrations.
- Focused real-dependency tests: **37 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** across `test_provider_lifecycle.py`, `test_provider_lifecycle_integration_db.py`, `test_shopify_adapter.py`, and `test_woocommerce_adapter.py`. Native tests connected as `ceopro_app` and verified FORCE RLS, non-superuser/non-BYPASSRLS, cross-tenant invisibility, direct-write denial, duplicate/reconnect behavior, source-change fencing, audit evidence, hard-delete restriction, receipt lease fencing, checkpoint idempotency/version updates, signed Shopify delivery persistence, duplicate/conflicting replay, and safe state transitions.
- Synthetic provider fixtures only. No real merchant credentials, live provider requests, webhook registrations, production activation, canonical commerce writes, usage/Credit mutations, or privacy deletion were performed.
- Isolated temporary environment installed `psycopg2-binary`, `pytest`, `httpx` and its dependencies; no production dependency manifest changed.

### Remaining boundaries

- WooCommerce binding/reconnect remains inactive until its authenticated verification path supplies a stable store/account ID under an approved least-privilege contract. A supplied URL is not treated as identity.
- Shopify public/custom app model, OAuth/offline token lifecycle, scopes, credential provisioning and live verification remain O-PROVIDER gates. This batch adds a service-level bind helper, not an OAuth route or public customer activation path.
- Checkpoint storage and the transactional completion hook exist, but neither WooCommerce nor Shopify has a scheduled receipt-bound worker wired to pass provider stream checkpoints. Provider paging/cursor semantics and live retry certification remain open.
- The Shopify delivery verifier and durable receipt primitive are implemented, but there is no public webhook receiver/registration/ack route or live delivery certification. WooCommerce's signature helper is not yet connected to durable source-attributed receipts.
- Shopify privacy requests remain in-memory authenticated helper results: migration 94 intentionally excludes privacy request receipt/fence structures. Durable intake/reimport protection, field mapping and destructive processing remain incomplete; no erasure is claimed.
- O-IMPORT/O-SOURCE approvals authorize provider namespaces and source/location ownership protection only. Migration 94 does not add product/variant/order/item mappings, inventory observation/location persistence, typed drafts or review workflows. Canonical commerce writes remain disabled.
- Customer connected-source counts and rights were not changed. Hard tenant deletion will now be blocked while provider bindings/checkpoints/deliveries remain; the future tenant-erasure workflow must explicitly handle that evidence.

This advances the provider lifecycle foundation but does not make WooCommerce or Shopify production-operational and does not complete Phases 3–5. Store scheduling, staging/review, identity mappings, inventory/order reconciliation, privacy handling and live provider certification remain prerequisites for production activation.

## Provider record identity and reviewed association — Migration 95 — 2026-10-21

The owner explicitly authorized Migration 95 and its implementation on 2026-10-21. Migration `20261028000700_provider_record_identity.sql` is the next chronological migration after M94. Migrations 1–94 remain unchanged. O-IMPORT, O-SOURCE, U6, and the approved safe PRIVACY-001 foundation are incorporated as approved; this migration implements only the product/variant/order/line identity and reviewed-association portion of O-IMPORT.

### Schema and enforcement

- Added `keel_provider_record_identities`, uniquely keyed by tenant, M94 verified source, provider, entity type, and provider record ID. Provider IDs remain source-namespaced; Shopify GIDs are entity-typed and WooCommerce IDs are positive decimal provider IDs. Product names, SKUs, and barcodes are not identity keys.
- Added same-tenant/source/provider parent and related-identity constraints for variants, orders and line items; composite links preserve optional ingestion-job provenance and optional canonical product, transaction or invoice references. Canonical targets are association references only; no canonical write or promotion occurs.
- Added `keel_provider_record_revisions` for append-only, deduplicated revision fingerprints and `keel_provider_record_reviews` for one immutable explicit association decision with database-derived reviewer identity and safe reason/evidence metadata.
- New tables have FORCE RLS. `ceopro_app` receives tenant-filtered SELECT only; narrowly scoped SECURITY DEFINER observe/associate functions use a hardened search path, trusted tenant/actor context, verified M94 source binding, active-principal checks, existing tenant owner/admin review roles, concurrency locks and explicit EXECUTE grants. Direct application-role writes are denied.
- Restrictive foreign keys protect tenant, source, parent identity, revisions, review actor, and canonical targets from hard deletion while dependent evidence exists. Disconnection/deactivation does not delete identity evidence. No cascade, anonymization, retention timer, backfill, PII, raw provider payload, credential or accounting record was added.
- On duplicate observations, identical versioned revisions are idempotent; a strictly newer timestamp can advance non-reviewed revision metadata; stale observations do not replace newer revisions; equal-time incompatible revisions or changed parent/provider references mark the identity conflict while retaining any reviewed canonical association. Repeated observations without any provider revision marker are treated as ambiguous and held as conflict because unchanged content cannot be proven without storing provider payloads. Missing provider IDs fail before persistence.

### Python integration and boundaries

`src/market_scraper/provider_identity.py` consumes existing Shopify and WooCommerce adapter records and persists only provider IDs, parent/related provider references, revision metadata/fingerprints and optional ingestion-job identity. It does not persist source payloads/customer information, create new connections, change capacity, write canonical business data, reconcile order/refund semantics, update inventory, or expose a new public review/admin route. Internal association persistence is available only through the existing tenant owner/admin database authorization contract; no reviewer ID is accepted from the caller. A WooCommerce numeric ID normalization defect discovered by native tests was corrected so the helper safely accepts its own canonical decimal representation on repeated normalization.

### Verification evidence

- Fresh disposable PostgreSQL 17/pgvector install applied migrations 1–95; a repeat run applied 0 migrations.
- Populated disposable 94→95 upgrade passed with seeded company, `data_sources`, product and verified provider-source counts unchanged (`1,1,1,1`); repeat applied 0 migrations.
- `test_provider_identity.py`: 7 passed using actual restricted `ceopro_app`; covered RLS/direct-write denial, provider/source namespace separation, concurrent replay, product/variant/order/line persistence, reviewed and idempotent association, conflict preservation for incompatible and unversioned records, cross-source parent rejection, cross-tenant invisibility/association denial, PII non-persistence, and tenant/source/parent/canonical deletion restrictions.
- `test_provider_lifecycle_integration_db.py`: 6 passed against the database at M95, preserving M94 registration/checkpoint/delivery behavior.
- Existing `test_shopify_adapter.py` and `test_woocommerce_adapter.py`: 25 passed, confirming the read-only provider adapter contracts remain compatible.
- Tests used synthetic fixtures and disposable services only; no live provider certification or production activation is claimed. Subscription-capacity and usage/Credit rows were not changed by migration 95 or its helpers; provider sync activation remains disabled.

### Remaining boundaries

M95 does not implement inventory location/ownership observations, typed import drafts, canonical product/order/invoice promotion, refunds/order revisions, webhook/privacy persistence, Shopify OAuth, or live provider activation. Those remain in later separately authorized schema/contract batches. Provider-specific identity verification and app/credential flows remain O-PROVIDER gated. Existing canonical associations require explicit review and cannot be overwritten by later syncs. No Migration 96 work is started.

## Inventory source/location provenance and authority selection — Migration 96 — 2026-10-21

The owner explicitly approved Migration 96, source/location inventory provenance, tenant authority selection, and restrictive evidence deletion on 2026-10-21. Migration `20261028000800_inventory_source_location_authority.sql` follows M95. Migrations 1–95 remain unchanged.

### Implementation

- Added `keel_inventory_locations` as tenant-defined canonical location identities; `keel_provider_inventory_locations` and append-only `keel_provider_inventory_location_observations` retain verified Shopify location IDs, display-name/status revisions, and observation evidence. WooCommerce receives no fabricated physical location because its configured REST product contract exposes product-level stock fields, not provider location IDs.
- Added append-only `keel_inventory_observations`, keyed by tenant/source/provider and a supplied observation digest which is checked against the complete observation on replay. Rows preserve exact quantity semantics (`available`, `on_hand`, or `woocommerce_stock_quantity`), numeric quantity, source-scoped provider product/inventory IDs, optional M95 product/variant identity, optional location, revision/time, ingestion job, ENT-008 receipt, and reconciliation state. Same-provider/same-revision quantity disagreements are retained and the new observation is marked `conflict`; identical replay is idempotent. No SKU/name matching, quantity conversion, aggregation, or automatic stale/superseded classification occurs.
- Added append-only `keel_inventory_location_decisions` and `keel_inventory_authority_decisions`. Existing tenant owner/admin authorization is checked in hardened database functions and actor identity is derived from session context. Location association and authority changes use per-key advisory locks, explicit sequence numbers, prior-decision references, and audit rows. Provider authority selection requires an M95 reviewed product/variant association, a verified active M94 source, an explicitly associated location where applicable, and an observation for that exact source/product/field/location. Manual authority is represented only for the existing canonical `stock_quantity` field.
- All six new tables use FORCE RLS and tenant-filtered SELECT for `ceopro_app`; direct application-role writes are revoked. Narrow SECURITY DEFINER functions have a fixed search path, trusted tenant/actor checks, explicit EXECUTE grants, and no public mutation route. Composite tenant/source/product/identity/job/receipt foreign keys use RESTRICT. Append-only observations and decision records reject UPDATE/DELETE; provider disconnection does not remove them.
- Added `src/market_scraper/inventory_provenance.py` to persist Shopify location and `available`/`on_hand` observations and WooCommerce explicitly managed `stock_quantity` observations, plus authorized internal location/authority decisions and safe tenant-context-bound history retrieval. It writes no canonical inventory. The existing provider adapters have no scheduled synchronization caller, so this module is an integration boundary and does not make unattended inventory synchronization operational.

- Focused existing provider identity/lifecycle and Shopify/WooCommerce adapter suites, plus new inventory unit tests: **35 passed, 18 skipped**. Skips include live-database tests because no `AI_TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`APP_DB_PASSWORD` is configured.

The repository migration runner applied the fresh base schema plus all **96** migrations; `schema_migrations` contained 96 rows and included M96. A second database was independently migrated through M1–95, seeded with a tenant, verified Shopify source, canonical product, stock quantity 37, and an explicitly reviewed M95 product identity, then upgraded by the same runner with M96. M96 applied once and the repeat run applied zero migrations. Counts for every pre-existing public table were unchanged except the expected new `schema_migrations` row; the seeded inventory quantity and reviewed M95 association were unchanged, and no M96 canonical inventory observation was created by the migration.
- The focused suite (`test_inventory_provenance.py`, `test_inventory_provenance_integration_db.py`, `test_provider_identity.py`, `test_provider_lifecycle.py`, `test_provider_lifecycle_integration_db.py`, `test_shopify_adapter.py`, and `test_woocommerce_adapter.py`) ran on both databases: **53 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped on the fresh chain** and **53 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped on the populated upgrade**. This includes actual restricted `ceopro_app` RLS and FORCE RLS checks, direct-write denial, tenant isolation, owner/staff authorization, duplicate and concurrent replay, authority audit/revoke history, conflict retention, deletion protection, M94/M95 regressions, and unchanged canonical stock. Credentials were random, process-local, and used only for the disposable container. Docker container and temporary databases were removed after the run.

The populated upgrade comparison verified all pre-existing public table row counts, including any present subscription, usage, and Credit rows, remained unchanged. Shopify `available` and `on_hand` remain distinct. WooCommerce `stock_quantity` remains product/variation-scoped with no location. Canonical stock mutation, aggregation, cross-source identity changes, and automatic deletion remain disabled. Migration acceptance is verified on a real fresh 1–96 PostgreSQL 17/pgvector 0.8.7 chain and a separately populated 1–95 to 96 upgrade; both repeat runs applied zero migrations. M96 does not complete typed import drafts, invoice/order reconciliation, provider OAuth/registration, or production sync activation. Phase 6 provider/live release verification remains open.

This checkpoint is verified locally and is intended for a scoped local commit after final diff review. Upstream comparison remains stale at `origin/main=c8c4b77ae5a9ec7a77ed105256fa5316aa09fa90`; the previous proxy/FETCH_HEAD issue prevented safe refresh. No upstream changes were merged or copied.

## Typed import drafts and review — Migration 97 — 2026-10-21

The owner approved Migration 97's typed import draft/review schema and restrictive deletion protection on 2026-10-21. Migration `20261028000900_typed_import_drafts_review.sql` follows M96. Migrations 1–96 are unchanged.

### Implementation

- Added `keel_import_batches`, `keel_import_revisions`, `keel_import_draft_rows`, and `keel_import_review_decisions`. Composite tenant/source/job/staging/revision/row/reviewer keys keep records within one tenant and preserve source provenance. Batches carry an idempotency key and request fingerprint; revisions and rows are immutable; amendments append a new revision; review records append the authenticated actor, decision, reason code, prior decision, and timestamp.
- Added tenant-scoped validation and draft operations in `backend/src/ai/extraction/typed_imports.py` for sales, invoice, and inventory categories. Values are allowlisted, numeric/date/currency fields are checked, invalid rows and ambiguity findings are retained, and provider product/location candidates without an established association remain `review_required`. The service derives actor/tenant authority from the authenticated database context. Owner/admin amendments and decisions go through hardened, narrowly granted SQL functions; `ceopro_app` has SELECT-only table grants and FORCE RLS on all four tables.
- Added transaction-scoped advisory locking for concurrent first-time batch identity creation. Review retries return the original decision after its batch is final; new decisions still require the current open revision. Revision creation is idempotent and preserves earlier reviewed evidence. Hard tenant/source/job/staging/reviewer deletes are restricted while evidence depends on them; direct draft/revision/row/decision writes and destructive mutations by `ceopro_app` are denied.
- Wired the existing extraction summary into typed sales draft persistence in `extraction_upload`. The established `promotion.promote_ingested_rows` path remains the only existing canonical sales-promotion authority and its API response contract is unchanged. Invoice and inventory batch persistence is available through the internal typed service and validated natively, but no new public review endpoint or frontend contract was introduced. Review acceptance never posts invoices, writes inventory, or creates a financial transaction.
- No raw uploaded file, credential, secret, or unrelated PII is copied into the new evidence tables. No migration backfill, billing, usage, entitlement, subscription, Credit, catalog, or canonical inventory policy was added.

### Verification evidence

- A disposable Docker `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` database ran PostgreSQL 17 with pgvector 0.8.7. The repository migration runner applied the fresh base schema and migrations 1–97; `schema_migrations` held 97 entries, and a repeat applied zero. A separate database ran the same runner through M1–M96, was seeded with a tenant, source, product, stock quantity 37, and M96 authority evidence, then ran M97 once; the repeat applied zero. The seeded tenant/source/product and M96 evidence counts and canonical stock quantity were unchanged by M97. Both database containers were removed after testing.
- Focused suite on each database: **115 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. It included `test_typed_imports.py`, `test_typed_imports_integration_db.py`, M96 inventory provenance tests, M95 provider identity tests, M94 provider lifecycle tests, extraction staging and promotion tests, plus their native database regressions. Native checks ran as restricted `ceopro_app` for RLS, cross-tenant isolation, direct-write denial, authorized owner/admin review, staff rejection, immutable revisions/rows/decisions, source/tenant deletion protection, concurrent duplicate submission, review/revision retries, invoice/inventory draft-only behavior, and unchanged canonical inventory.
- The acceptance run did not execute the live HTTP upload endpoint, frontend review workflow, large-file stress, worker crash/restart, grouped invoice posting, provider-order reconciliation, or live provider certification. The extraction-summary persistence helper was directly integration-tested with an existing staged row. These remain separate work/release gates; no claim of invoice/inventory promotion or full API/UI review support is made.
- Test defects found during verification were fixed before final evidence: concurrent batch creation now serializes absent-key creation; committed review retries are idempotent after final state; revision SQL qualifies output-column names. The inventory draft test fixture now seeds canonical stock so the no-write assertion is meaningful. Final focused runs passed on both databases after these corrections.

### Acceptance and remaining boundaries

`AT-IMPORT-001`, `003–007`, and `009–012` now record M97 validation, draft, review, provenance, integration, and native test evidence. The sales draft is stored alongside existing staging before the established sales promotion path. Invoice and inventory records remain drafts; review-only approval does not authorize canonical writes. At this schema checkpoint, grouped invoice/order reconciliation, invoice/tax/payment/refund semantics, public preview/review APIs, frontend flow, connector-to-draft execution for provider invoices/inventory, and release HTTP/worker/provider certification remained open. The operational API checkpoint below supersedes the preview/review API status. Existing source identity and inventory ownership continue to rely on M94–M96. No Migration 98 is started.

## Migration 97 operational API checkpoint — 2026-10-21

This follow-up wires the existing M97 records and review functions to authenticated tenant HTTP operations. No migration was added; migrations 1–97 remain unchanged.

### API behavior

- `GET /extraction/import-drafts?limit=&offset=&category=&state=` returns paginated tenant batch metadata, source name/type, ingestion status, row validation counts, and canonical commit counts. Limit is bounded to 1–100.
- `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}` returns the current revision preview, allowlisted typed values, field findings, identity candidates, row review result, source/job context, and an explicit canonical posting status per row.
- `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/revisions` lists immutable revisions; `GET .../revisions/{revision_id}` previews an earlier immutable revision.
- `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/reviews?revision_id=` returns append-only decision history with internal actor IDs and reason codes; it does not return actor profile data.
- `POST /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/reviews` accepts `revision_id`, `decision` (`approve`/`reject`), `idempotency_key`, `reason_code`, and optional `row_id`. Tenant/actor come from the authenticated request and restricted DB context. The M97 database function enforces owner/admin review rights, current-revision fencing, idempotency and row scope. Stale revisions return 409, cross-tenant/unknown batches return 404, unauthenticated calls return 401, and unauthorized reviewers return 403.
- Existing `GET /extraction/imports` remains unchanged. `POST /extraction/upload` has an additive `import_draft` response object containing the persisted batch/revision IDs and the explicit `review_only` / `canonical_write_performed_by_review=false` flags.

Review state and canonical posting state are separate. A review approval records a decision only; invoice drafts stay unposted, inventory drafts do not write stock, and review does not merge products. Existing approved sales promotion still runs during upload for rows admitted by the established promotion pipeline. Its staging `COMMITTED` link is shown as already committed; the later review action does not perform or repeat that write. Existing conflicting-name/barcode ambiguity checks and duplicate content-derived invoice protection were kept intact. A broad proposal to hold every unique existing product match would have changed established sales promotion, so that wider behavior change was not made.

### Verification

- Unit/regression: `test_main.py`, `test_import_drafts_api.py`, `test_typed_imports.py`, and `test_extraction_job_status.py`: **128 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** on the isolated Python environment. Warnings were existing TestClient/httpx deprecation, JWT test-key length, and a `job_management.py` docstring escape warning.
- Native disposable PostgreSQL 17 / pgvector 0.8.7: the real migration runner applied the fresh 1–97 chain; a second runner invocation succeeded with zero additional migrations. Authenticated HTTP upload, list, preview, revision view, review, replay, staff rejection, 401, cross-tenant 404, and invoice/inventory review-only actions ran through the restricted `ceopro_app` connection. Existing M97 restricted-role review/RLS tests, sales promotion SQL regressions, and upload pool lifecycle tests were included. Final: **40 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**.
- A 501-row HTTP upload crossed the existing 500-row extraction commit boundary and returned all 501 draft rows with a truthful completed job state. An invalid quantity remained in the preview with field findings; the `ingestion_jobs` status uses its supported `FAILED` value while preserving partial row counts and staging findings. Replaying a review returned the original decision; transaction count did not change after review. Re-uploading identical sales content preserved existing canonical invoice deduplication, but each distinct HTTP POST creates a new ingestion job and M97 batch. The current API/schema has no stable request-level upload idempotency identity, so a network retry cannot be distinguished from an intentional re-import; this acceptance item is partial and remains a production gap. A future narrowly scoped migration may be needed for tenant/source-scoped upload request receipts (idempotency key plus request digest and job/batch result), without changing canonical accounting. No such migration is authorized or created here.
- No dedicated durable CSV upload worker, job-claim route, or crash-resume API exists in this path. The upload request processes synchronously while existing ingestion commits in bounded chunks. Worker crash/restart and resuming a partially completed HTTP request were therefore not claimed as tested or newly implemented. M97 same-job draft/review retry and duplicate creation primitives remain covered by native tests. Storage of a re-uploaded source file is optional and not a basis for a new recovery contract.
- Native database fixture used a disposable container and real dependencies; environment credentials were test-only and removed, and the new container was removed. No customer or shared database was used. No migration, frontend code, billing/Credit rule, or canonical invoice/inventory write was introduced.

### Frontend integration notes

The existing `Recent Data Activity` view can consume the legacy `/extraction/imports` endpoint unchanged. A later frontend batch can add: a recent typed-draft list using `/extraction/import-drafts`; a row preview showing typed fields, validation findings and candidate/ambiguity reasons; revision and reviewer decision history; and separate reviewed versus canonically committed indicators. Owner/admin actions can call the review POST and show the returned review status. The UI must say that an approved invoice/inventory review is still a draft and must not promise posting or stock synchronization.

### Migration 98 boundary

No Migration 98 is required for the preview/review API itself. Stable request-level idempotency across separate HTTP upload POSTs is a distinct open requirement because a retry currently creates a new ingestion job and M97 batch; a narrow tenant/source-scoped request-receipt migration may be required if the owner wants durable retry identity. Existing `invoices`, `invoice_items`, and `transactions` remain the canonical financial authorities; M95 `keel_provider_record_identities` already stores provider order/line identities and optional canonical invoice/transaction targets; M97 stores typed drafts and reviews. Grouped invoice/order reconciliation remains a separate conditional schema proposal: if a reviewed reconciliation contract needs durable group/line links not expressible by those existing keys, add one tenant/source-scoped group table linking a M97 batch/revision and M95 provider order identity to an existing invoice, plus a line-link table from M97 draft rows/M95 order-line identities to existing invoice items/transactions, with unique source identities, composite tenant FKs, FORCE RLS, restricted audited writes and append-only reconciliation events. It must not duplicate invoice totals or payment state. Neither proposal is approved or required by this API batch. Invoice numbering, item grouping, fractional quantities, tax, discount allocation, payment/settlement, refund, cancellation and provider order revision semantics remain decisions/blockers before any posting implementation.
## M97 import-review frontend checkpoint — 2026-10-21

The existing `/connect-data` page now includes a typed-import history and review panel backed by the already implemented M97 APIs. No migration or backend API change was made; migrations 1–97 remain unchanged.

### User workflow

- Lists tenant import drafts with offset pagination, sales/invoice/inventory filtering, source, creation time, validation/review counts, review state, and canonical-posting state. Loading, empty, retry, and API error states are provided.
- Opens the selected draft for typed row preview with findings adjacent to the affected row, identity ambiguity messaging, revision selection, review history, and row pagination (25 rows per page). Product/location IDs are not displayed; only whether a reference exists is shown. The UI uses the backend's allowlisted typed values and fields.
- Owner/admin controls follow the existing role utility and are additionally enforced by backend authorization. Review decisions require an explicit reason and confirmation, disable controls while pending, retain the same request idempotency key after an uncertain response, refresh after server confirmation, and show 401/403/404/409/422-specific feedback. Historical revisions are read-only.
- The interface prominently states that review is not posting. Invoice approval does not create/settle a canonical invoice; inventory approval does not change stock; sales already committed by the existing import path are not posted again by review. Review history omits reviewer IDs and shows decision, time, and reason.
- The existing upload action invalidates the draft list after a successful upload. English and Arabic labels were added to the existing locale bundles. Responsive table wrappers, keyboard focus, labeled regions, semantic table headers, dialog confirmation, and status/error roles use the existing design components and styles.

### API and verification

The panel consumes the actual backend routes: `GET /extraction/import-drafts`, `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}`, `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/revisions`, `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/revisions/{revision_id}`, `GET /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/reviews`, and `POST /extraction/import-drafts/{batch_id}/reviews`. The client sends the implemented `revision_id`, `decision`, `idempotency_key`, `reason_code`, and optional `row_id`; authenticated tenant and actor authority continue to come from the server.

- Frontend suite: **118 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. This includes API contract/request encoding, error mapping, component server-render smoke with semantic table/focus-region assertions, existing extraction/upload regressions, and English/Arabic locale parity.
- `npm run lint`: **passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed** (Vite production build, 2,965 modules transformed).
- Browser E2E and browser axe were **not run**: this checkout has no authenticated E2E account configured and no local frontend/backend listeners on ports 5173/8000. Thus upload-to-preview and review against a live disposable database, 401/403/409 browser handling, and responsive visual behavior remain unverified in a browser. No production or shared service was contacted.

### Remaining limits

- Distinct upload POST retries still create separate ingestion jobs/batches; client button state cannot establish server-side upload idempotency. No new schema was added.
- The existing upload request remains synchronous and has no crash-resume worker contract; this frontend does not claim upload recovery after a process interruption.
- Review approval remains review-only for invoice and inventory drafts. Grouped invoice/order reconciliation, tax/payment/refund semantics, canonical inventory updates, and frontend flows for those downstream operations remain out of scope.
- The UI exposes no upload retry identity and makes no claim that a reviewed invoice, inventory draft, or ambiguous product has been canonically posted.


## Operational commerce connector-to-evidence integration ? 2026-10-21

### Implementation

- Each stream's evidence writes, completed ingestion job, `last_synced_at`, and M94 checkpoint advance share one transaction guarded by the current ENT-008 lease and source credential/configuration fingerprint. Checkpoints currently record completed full scans with an empty continuation because the Shopify adapter consumes GraphQL cursors but does not expose its final cursor to the worker. Retried runs repeat the full bounded read; M95/M96 writes are idempotent.
- Expired provider-started receipts return to retryable only for active, policy-ALLOWED, M94-verified Shopify reads handled by this worker. It performs read-only GraphQL and idempotent M95/M96 persistence. Other uncertain provider operations retain `reconciliation_required`. Safe retry reopens the receipt's own FAILED ingestion job under the current pre-provider lease instead of creating an unattached replacement. Failed evidence persistence does not advance a checkpoint.
- WooCommerce is discovered by the scheduler but blocked before receipt creation/provider access unless stable verified M94 identity exists. Its current Python verification confirms REST access only and cannot safely bind a store.

## Shopify worker native verification and transaction hardening — 2026-10-22

### Native verification

- Disposable PostgreSQL 17 with pgvector 0.8.7 ran the real migration runner on an empty database through migrations 1–97. A second invocation applied **0 migrations**. The focused suite below used the restricted ceopro_app role for application paths and confirmed FORCE RLS. No migration file was changed; no schema migration is needed for these corrections.
- Focused suite: **122 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** across provider synchronization, Shopify GraphQL adapter, M94 lifecycle/receipts, M95 identities, M96 inventory, M97 typed imports, generic connector and API-worker regressions.
- The native Shopify worker test dispatches through the existing connector scheduler/worker. It verifies the synthetic authenticated provider identity/scopes fixture, tenant/source attribution, product/variant/order/line identities, location and named inventory quantities, duplicates, source-level receipt/job reuse, and atomic completion of all three streams.
- A provider read failure or evidence persistence failure does not advance any checkpoint. A failed multi-row persistence rolls back its earlier row. Retry reuses the failed job/receipt and idempotently records evidence. In-flight credential rotation invalidates the source fence; the stale worker writes no evidence, completion or checkpoint.
- Canonical products, stock, transactions, invoices, usage counters and usage ledger remained unchanged. The test confirmed current role ceopro_app, no superuser/BYPASSRLS, and FORCE RLS on provider identities. No credits, charges, live merchant credentials or production data were used.

### Production corrections

- Fixed malformed SQL in expired-receipt recovery that prevented PostgreSQL from dispatching the scheduler recovery path.
- Restricted the provider-binding lookup row lock to the mutable data_sources row. M94 intentionally grants the application role SELECT-only access to provider-binding evidence; the old lock requested a stronger privilege and failed under ceopro_app.
- Changed Shopify scheduling from one ENT-008 source receipt per stream to one source-level receipt/job for products, orders and inventory. All evidence writes, successful job status and three stream checkpoints commit together under lease/configuration fencing. This prevents one stream's retryable receipt from being reused as another stream's completed job.
- The Shopify checkpoint is explicitly a **completed full scan** with an empty continuation. The adapter consumes page cursors internally; incremental cursor handoff and resume are not implemented. A fetch is not canonical promotion.

### Provider boundaries and remaining work

- Shopify worker execution is database-backed and native-tested only with synthetic preverified source bindings and contract-shaped provider fixtures. Shopify OAuth/distribution model, live registration, token lifecycle, merchant activation and live certification remain inactive and owner/Phase 6 gated.
- WooCommerce now preserves the original URL + Consumer Key/Secret onboarding model. The URL is bound as the logical source namespace only after authenticated wc/v3 product, order, and currency reads succeed; it is not treated as proof on its own. A provider-issued immutable installation ID is not exposed by the verified contract, so URL aliases remain distinct. No companion plugin is required for this compatible path.
- Provider product/order payload values still lack a compatible typed M97 category; current durable evidence is provider identity/revision (M95) and supported inventory observations (M96), not canonical product/order staging. Grouped invoice/order/refund reconciliation, webhook receiver activation, privacy processing and canonical commerce writes remain blocked.
- M97 browser E2E and server-side idempotency for separate upload POST retries remain outstanding. This batch adds store connection forms only; it does not alter the M97 review frontend.
- Upstream metadata refresh remains unavailable through the configured proxy. Last locally known origin/main is c8c4b77ae5a9ec7a77ed105256fa5316aa09fa90; teammate comparison may be stale.
- Upstream metadata refresh was attempted with read-only git ls-remote and failed because the configured proxy at 127.0.0.1:9 is unavailable. The last known origin/main is c8c4b77ae5a9ec7a77ed105256fa5316aa09fa90; teammate comparison may be stale. No upstream changes were merged or copied.

## Node store-connection compatibility corrections — 2026-10-22

### Connection-flow comparison

| Flow | Existing Node behavior | Python state before this batch | Compatibility result |
|---|---|---|---|
| WooCommerce | Tenant owner/admin connects a public HTTPS store URL with WooCommerce Consumer Key/Secret. KEEL tests authenticated REST access, encrypts the credentials, and reconnects by the existing store/source identity while enforcing connected-source capacity. | Python had an adapter and M94–M96 evidence helpers, but no compatible onboarding or worker path. | Python onboarding now accepts the same URL + ck_/cs_ contract, performs bounded authenticated wc/v3 product, order, and currency reads before M94 binding, stores keys in the existing vault, reconnects to the verified URL namespace, and applies existing capacity checks only for a new or reactivated source. No companion WordPress plugin is required. The URL alone is never treated as proof; it is bound only after provider API verification. URL aliases are not silently consolidated. |
| Shopify install | Node uses signed tenant/user/shop state with a one-use nonce, verifies the callback signature, exchanges the authorization code, verifies shop GID and scopes, and returns a short-lived ticket to the initiating authenticated KEEL session before encrypted credential storage. | Python had a GraphQL client and provider worker but lacked the OAuth installation flow. | Python now ports the same state/callback/ticket/session-bound exchange model, verifies shop identity and required scopes, stores credentials in the existing vault, and binds/reconnects through M94 by verified Shop GID. This local integration does not require Shopify App Store publication. |
| Store sync | Node schedules WooCommerce and Shopify reads and applies its existing connector lifecycle and canonical behavior. | Python had durable ENT-008 execution and M94–M97 evidence services, but scheduled provider authorization predicates and dispatch supported Shopify only. | Python scheduler and receipt authorization now support both provider bindings. Both workers reuse existing source configuration fencing, ENT-008 lease claims, M94 checkpoints, M95 identities and supported M96 observations. Results are full-scan evidence only; no unapproved canonical commerce promotion is enabled. |

### Implemented compatibility changes

- Added authenticated WooCommerce URL + REST-key onboarding, source reactivation/reconnect, capacity checks, credential vault storage, and verified M94 binding. Verification reads the actual wc/v3 products, orders and currency endpoints. The verified URL namespace is used only after those reads succeed.
- Added the Node-compatible Shopify signed OAuth state, one-time callback validation, short-lived callback ticket, initiating-user binding, authorization-code exchange, required-scope verification, M94 binding/reconnect, and encrypted vault persistence.
- Preserved Node's optional read_all_orders permission and the development-store-only test-order opt-in; default reads still exclude test orders.
- Connected WooCommerce into the existing provider scheduler, ENT-008 source receipts, M95 product/order identities, supported M96 inventory observations, truthful job result, and full-scan M94 checkpoints.
- Corrected ENT-008 SQL authorization and recovery predicates to authorize either Shopify or WooCommerce only when the provider binding matches the source collector. Unverified or mismatched bindings remain rejected.
- Extended existing database registration/credential helpers with optional transaction control so provider identity binding and vaulted credentials commit atomically with onboarding.
- Added the existing WooCommerce and Shopify connection forms to the Python repository's frontend and wired them to the actual Python onboarding endpoints. The original Node repository remained read-only.
- Removed two unnecessary architecture blockers from the local integration path: no companion WordPress plugin is required for the original verified Woo URL/key contract, and Shopify App Store publication is not required to implement or locally configure the existing OAuth install/callback flow. Live merchant operation still needs valid app/provider credentials and release certification.

## Authentication final acceptance closure ? 2026-10-22

- Fixed a real workspace-switch race in the shared React HTTP client: a delayed 401 from a revoked prior-workspace token no longer clears the newly selected session. The interceptor now compares the failed request token with the currently active bearer token.

- Session contract verified: default access-token lifetime 120 minutes; `/auth/refresh` renews the same bearer token, retains original `auth_time`, and enforces 168-hour maximum session age. This is not a distinct access/refresh token pair. Deterministic native tests reject over-age sessions; browser verified renewal and localized expired-session redirect with no loop.
- AUTH-013 is not a Python application defect: Python identity and current sessions remain supported. Node account/password/session bulk migration was neither authorized nor executed. Cross-repository identity inventory and customer cutover remain a separate owner-authorized deployment/migration gate; no account merge is claimed.

### Feature catalog crosswalk and owner decisions

The existing `FEATURE_AND_ENTITLEMENT_CROSSWALK.csv` retains all 21 Node feature codes and three separate Python-only rights. Of the 21, eleven map to established Python codes (`market_intelligence`, `demand_prediction`, `competitor_management`, `sentiment_analysis`, `rag_assistant`, `document_extraction`, `marketing_image_generation`, `tracked_competitors`, `tracked_products`, `connected_data_sources`, `document_storage_mb`); three remain candidate aliases only (`market_perception`→`market_intelligence`, `report_generation`→`monthly_reports`, `team_members`→`team_seats`); seven remain unmapped (`dashboard_analytics`, `inventory_intelligence`, `product_management`, `data_integration`, `business_recommendations`, `system_alerts`, `ai_pricing`). Python-only `data_credits`, `ai_credits` and `web_searches` remain separate. Established units remain as cataloged in that crosswalk; no alias or new right was activated.

For one coordinated owner decision round:

- **U5 — Rights, cohorts and grandfathering (ENT-001/002/006/011):** decide whether each of the three aliases is an approved alias or an independent right, and the per-plan/cohort/effective-date treatment for the seven unmapped codes and legacy missing rows. Options: keep unmapped/deny; approve explicit future-only mappings; or approve a bounded cohort migration after an impact report. Recommendation: keep the current deny/unmapped behavior and use future-only explicit catalog rows unless a documented existing contract proves a grandfathered grant. Impacts: no existing records change; any mapping/backfill can change access and source/seat capacity. Exact block: new gates, alias enforcement and historical rights backfill. Missing rows now fail closed and are distinct from an explicit `NULL` unlimited row.
- **U1 — Feature-metering units (ENT-003/004/010, CREDIT-006):** approve feature-specific unit, rounding, period and retained-output semantics for any new metered feature. Options: count requests, count measured output, or reserve/settle a declared input/output unit. Recommendation: preserve the current Python units and charge usable retained outputs only where those units are already explicit; do not extend metering until every new unit is stated. Impact: no balance changes from this batch. Exact block: activating metering for unmapped/new features and broad partial-output schedules.
- **U2/U3 — Uncertain outcomes and remedies (ENT-010, CREDIT-003/004/005):** define provider-outcome evidence, who may adjust a customer allowance/credit, ceilings, period and expiry for ambiguous delivery or later provider corrections. Options: keep conservative existing reservation/reconciliation behavior; allow evidence-bound bounded adjustments; or automatically release/refund ambiguous outcomes. Recommendation: retain existing conservative behavior until authoritative outcome evidence and bounded adjustment rights are approved. Impact: no automatic customer refund or additional charge. Exact block: new uncertain-delivery allowance remedies, adjustments and old-period exception handling.
- **U4 — Credit conversion and commercial terms (CREDIT-001/002/004):** decide whether to retain current cost-derived Credit model, define fixed conversions/top-ups/expiry, or provide another explicit commercial schedule. Recommendation: retain current separately configured Data/AI budgets and provider cost ledgers; keep top-ups/credits terms disabled until approved. Impact: existing balances and provider spend remain unchanged. Exact block: new conversions, top-ups, rollover/expiry changes and customer-facing adjustments.

Remaining acceptance statuses and this run's evidence are appended to the original ENT/CREDIT rows in `evidence/ACCEPTANCE_TEST_SPEC.csv`; original IDs, cases and assertions are unchanged.

### PLATFORM-002 server-side search closure follow-up — 2026-10-22

This follow-up started from `5152606a0951f32a080b33e952eadff647efa5ef`. Inspection confirmed that the current `/platform/companies` and `/platform/users` routes send only `limit`/`offset` to `keel_platform_command(action,p)`, whose authorized listing branches return a single page and have no search or count contract. The React page's existing search is therefore correctly labeled and limited to that loaded page. There is no safe application-only path to global filtering: the platform data is read through a restricted role and a SECURITY DEFINER platform permission function; adding a privileged direct query from `ceopro_app` would bypass that established authorization boundary. Migrations 1–98 remain immutable, and this task did not authorize Migration 99.

**Required separate authorization:** a forward-only Migration 99 containing one narrowly scoped, explicitly granted `SECURITY DEFINER` listing/search function (or equivalent authorized replacement) with fixed `search_path`, current active platform owner/admin validation from the authenticated database context, parameterized and escaped substring matching for only established company/user display fields, bounded limit and nonnegative offset validation, exact filtered count, deterministic `created_at DESC` plus unique-ID ordering, and additive response fields while preserving existing no-query response compatibility. No new tables or customer data backfill are needed. Following that authorization, implement the query parameter in both listing APIs and React; verify later-page matches, stable filtered pagination/count, invalid bounds, injection strings, tenant-owner denial, restricted `ceopro_app`, existing detail API compatibility, and Chromium English/Arabic search flows. Until then, `PLATFORM-002` remains **partial** and the prior page-local filter is not represented as global search. No new backend, frontend, migration, or test changes were made in this follow-up; no tests are claimed as executed for server-side search.


### PLATFORM-002 server-side search implementation - Migration 99 - 2026-10-22

The owner authorized Migration 99 directly for isolated local implementation. Added `20261028001100_platform_search_pagination.sql`; migrations 1-98 are unchanged. The migration installs `pg_trgm`, adds three targeted GIN trigram indexes over lowercased company names and user names/emails, and grants only `ceopro_app` EXECUTE on `public.keel_platform_search(text,text,integer,integer)`. The SECURITY DEFINER function has a fixed `search_path=public, pg_temp`, validates the requested resource/term/page bounds, checks the current active Platform Owner/Admin from the database actor context under the existing platform-authority advisory lock, records denials, escapes `\`, `%`, and `_` as literal search characters, and returns existing authorized fields with an exact filtered count. Ordering is stable (`created_at DESC`, UUID DESC). No table grants, new tables, sensitive fields, user/customer mutations, billing changes, or authorization changes were added. The pre-existing ceopro_app SELECT permissions on companies/users remain protected by FORCE RLS; native tests confirmed direct cross-tenant reads are denied. ceopro_app has no direct SELECT on platform membership/audit tables.

The companies/users HTTP listing routes accept an optional bounded `search` term. Existing callers that omit it retain the legacy `{items}` response; the additive search contract returns `{items,total,limit,offset}`. The React platform directory now debounces and sends search to the server, resets page and detail state when the term changes, shows filtered counts/loading/empty/error states, and paginates using the filtered total. English and Arabic labels/statuses preserve RTL behavior. Removed the old page-local-only filtering helper and its test.

**Executed verification:** Fresh disposable PostgreSQL 17.11/pgvector 0.8.7 applied migrations 1-99; official rerun applied 0. A separate disposable database applied migrations 1-98 through the runner, then was seeded with a tenant, user, membership and platform owner before the same runner applied M99; those records remained intact and the official rerun applied 0. Native platform/auth suite against the upgraded database and restricted `ceopro_app`: **48 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. It verifies database function ACL and fixed search path, no direct platform membership/audit grants, FORCE-RLS-scoped core table access with cross-tenant reads denied, owner/admin search, tenant-owner and suspended-operator denial, bounded API validation, injection/literal wildcard handling, 120-result filtered company and user searches with stable 50/50/20 pagination, later-page matches, empty queries/results and existing detail operations. Isolated FastAPI/React/Chromium run against the fresh database with Redis and controlled SMTP: **26 journeys passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, including company/user third-page results, filtered counts, details, empty results and Arabic RTL search. Frontend: **126 tests passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**; ESLint and Vite production build passed.

`PLATFORM-002` server-side search/filter/pagination is complete against the authorized database. This is local acceptance evidence, not production deployment/certification. `PLATFORM-003` catalog administration, `PLATFORM-004` exceptional financial execution and `PLATFORM-005` durable general notification delivery are unaffected.

## Feature Completion 06 — RAG Assistant and Knowledge Base — 2026-10-22

Starting checkpoint: `06d6404d2cebde1a561489765a7bfa91093cad2d` on `integration/keel-full-migration`. No schema change was required; migrations 1–99 remain unchanged. The disposable PostgreSQL 17.11/pgvector 0.8.7, Redis 7.4.11 and MinIO environment ran the existing migration chain fresh through 99 and a repeat run applied 0 migrations. No shared or production service was used.

- **Knowledge Base/business-data separation:** added authenticated `POST /rag/documents/upload` for the RAG-specific TXT, Markdown, PDF, DOCX and XLSX contract. It enforces existing size/type limits, tenant document capability, storage and extraction allowance, and uses tenant-scoped SHA-256 identity plus a database advisory lock for duplicate-upload idempotency. Files use the existing object store, RAG metadata and indexing path. This route does not create business ingestion jobs, sales, products, invoices or stock changes.
- **Truthful readiness and retrieval:** a document is searchable only when processing is complete and indexed chunks exist. Retrieval, missing-embedding selection and final citation-source selection filter for processed documents, preventing pending/failed records from appearing as retrieved evidence. Existing retrieval and model algorithms were not rewritten.
- **Indexing recovery and privacy-safe errors:** tenant-scoped PostgreSQL advisory locking prevents simultaneous ingestion workers from racing the same pending queue and releases on exit so interrupted work can retry. Failure logs omit filenames, file contents and parser exception text.
- **Chat recovery/history:** the user's question/session is committed before provider invocation. On unconfigured, unavailable or rate-limited provider outcomes, the API returns the persisted session ID and the UI restores it into history; unused token reservation is released. This fixes a prior mismatch where the UI said a failed question was retained although rollback had discarded it. No fabricated answer/citation is returned.
- **Frontend:** the existing Knowledge Base upload uses the dedicated RAG endpoint and KEEL upload component in Knowledge Base mode; business-import format behavior remains separate. The chat UI retains a saved failed question for retry/history. English readiness says “Ready for questions”; Arabic and RTL remain supported.

## Feature Completion - Products & Inventory Management - 2026-10-22

Starting checkpoint `6fdbc167eaab4a25603ff8b80e9143bfd277d89b`; work remained on `integration/keel-full-migration`. Existing migrations 1-99 are unchanged. The original Node repository and the separate Phase 1A worktree were not modified.

- Preserved the Python product catalog API and UI, adding server-side Owner/Admin enforcement for product create/edit/soft-delete and manual canonical stock updates. The server checks current `tenant_users.role_key` in the restricted tenant transaction with `FOR SHARE`; JWT role claims are not the write authority. The existing frontend now hides management actions for other roles and explains the restriction in English and Arabic. List and detail reads remain tenant-scoped; product capacity continues through the existing `TRACKED_PRODUCTS` authority.
- Manual inventory and initial quantities honor the approved `NUMERIC(14,4)` schema. UI and API validation allow up to four decimal places and do not round unit quantities. Provider observations remain source-owned M96 evidence and are not changed by manual canonical stock edits. The stock editor explains this boundary.
- Because the schema has no unique `(tenant_id, product_id)` inventory constraint, a manual inventory write locks its active parent product before insert-or-update. Normal application writes serialize on that product row. Product listing, dashboard stock summaries, and forecast inventory recommendations select at most the latest inventory row, preventing legacy duplicates from multiplying product metrics. This is defensive read behavior; legacy duplicate rows are not deleted or reconciled.
- A post-commit competitor-discovery notification is best-effort: if its optional extraction/Redis dependency fails after a product row is committed, the API logs the notification failure instead of returning a false product-create failure that can prompt duplicate retry. This does not claim the discovery job was queued.
- Focused product API/unit suite: **52 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. Frontend suite: **129 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**; ESLint and Vite build passed (2,972 modules). Authenticated Chromium passed **2 journeys** against local React and FastAPI test services: owner product edit/manual stock save and an actual cross-tenant product detail 404 in English desktop, plus Arabic RTL mobile without horizontal overflow. The backend process predated the final server-side role guard; this verifies the established owner path and RLS read boundary, not the new staff-denial path.
- Native PostgreSQL: nine dashboard/inventory integration assertions passed in the final focused run. Four product restricted-role cases could not establish `ceopro_app` connections because the test shell lacked the separately configured `APP_DB_PASSWORD`; these were setup/authentication errors, not assertion failures.
